# MASTER DEEP-RESEARCH METAPROMPT
# KAHIYE — AI-NATIVE PARENTING INTELLIGENCE OS
#
# Pregnancy → Birth → Infancy → Childhood → Adolescence → Age 18
#
# Deep Research → Developmental Science → Parenting Science →
# Child Psychology → Communication → Emotional Intelligence →
# Family Systems → Behavioral Intelligence → Safety →
# Longitudinal Memory → Personalization → Agent Architecture →
# Knowledge Graph → Product Intelligence
#
# ================================================================
# 00. MISSION
# ================================================================
#
# Conduct an exhaustive, current, multidisciplinary DEEP RESEARCH
# PROGRAM to design the intelligence architecture, knowledge system,
# safety architecture and specialist AI agents for:
#
#                       KAHIYE.APP
#
# Kahiye is intended to become a next-generation AI-native parenting
# intelligence platform supporting a family continuously from:
#
# PREGNANCY
# ↓
# BIRTH
# ↓
# INFANCY
# ↓
# TODDLERHOOD
# ↓
# EARLY CHILDHOOD
# ↓
# MIDDLE CHILDHOOD
# ↓
# PRE-ADOLESCENCE
# ↓
# ADOLESCENCE
# ↓
# AGE 18.
#
# Kahiye should become a longitudinal:
#
# PARENTING COMPANION
# +
# DEVELOPMENTAL INTELLIGENCE SYSTEM
# +
# CHILD-BEHAVIOR INTERPRETER
# +
# EMOTIONAL-INTELLIGENCE COACH
# +
# PARENT COMMUNICATION COACH
# +
# FAMILY RELATIONSHIP INTELLIGENCE SYSTEM
# +
# ANTICIPATORY GUIDANCE ENGINE
# +
# LIFE-SKILLS CURRICULUM
# +
# PARENT MEMORY SYSTEM
# +
# SAFETY / ESCALATION SYSTEM.
#
# This is NOT simply:
#
# "ChatGPT for parenting."
#
# The desired transformation is:
#
# QUESTION
# → CONTEXT
# → DEVELOPMENTAL UNDERSTANDING
# → CHILD NEED
# → PARENT RESPONSE
# → COMMUNICATION
# → ACTION
# → OBSERVATION
# → LEARNING
# → LONGITUDINAL MEMORY
# → BETTER FUTURE GUIDANCE.

# visual hierarchy.

# CURRENT-STATE PRODUCT MAP.
#
# Then classify every recommendation:
#
# KEEP
# IMPROVE
# EXTEND
# MERGE
# REPLACE
# NEW.
#
# Do NOT redesign Kahiye as an unrelated application.
#
# ================================================================
# 02. CORE PRODUCT PHILOSOPHY
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should help parents answer five recurring questions:
#
# WHAT IS HAPPENING?
#
# WHY MIGHT MY CHILD BE DOING THIS?
#
# IS THIS DEVELOPMENTALLY EXPECTED?
#
# WHAT SHOULD I DO RIGHT NOW?
#
# WHAT SHOULD I HELP MY CHILD LEARN OVER TIME?
#
# ================================================================
# 03. THE CENTRAL LOOP
# ================================================================
#
# OBSERVE
# ↓
# UNDERSTAND
# ↓
# RESPOND
# ↓
# CONNECT
# ↓
# TEACH
# ↓
# OBSERVE OUTCOME
# ↓
# LEARN.
#
# Kahiye should strengthen the parent's ability to perform this loop
# independently.
#
# ================================================================
# 04. PARENT CAPABILITY > AI DEPENDENCY
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye succeeds when parents gradually become better at:
#
# reading behavior
# recognizing emotion
# regulating themselves
# communicating
# setting boundaries
# repairing conflict
# supporting autonomy
# recognizing developmental change.
#
# It should NOT train:
#
# "Ask AI before responding to my child."
#

# EVIDENCE

# 13. DEVELOPMENTAL STAGE MODEL
# ================================================================
#
# Research a more granular model than:
#
# 0–2
# 3–6
# 7–12
# 13–18.
#
# Candidate structure:
#
# PREGNANCY — WEEK SPECIFIC
#
# NEWBORN — 0–3 MONTHS
#
# YOUNG INFANT — 3–6 MONTHS
#
# OLDER INFANT — 6–12 MONTHS
#
# YOUNG TODDLER — 12–24 MONTHS
#
# OLDER TODDLER — 2–3 YEARS
#
# PRESCHOOL — 3–5
#
# EARLY SCHOOL — 5–7
#
# MIDDLE CHILDHOOD — 7–9
#
# LATE CHILDHOOD — 9–12
#
# EARLY ADOLESCENCE — 12–14
#
# MIDDLE ADOLESCENCE — 14–16
#
# LATE ADOLESCENCE — 16–18.
#
# Research the scientifically useful boundaries rather than blindly
# adopting these.
#
# ================================================================
# 14. AGE IS NOT DESTINY
# ================================================================
#
# Never say:
#
# "At exactly 4 years and 12 days, curiosity increases."
#
# Development is variable.
#
# Replace false precision with:
#
# developmental windows
# ranges
# probabilities
# individual variation.
#

# 26. BEHAVIOR INTERPRETER
# ================================================================
#
# Design:
#
# KAHIYE BEHAVIOR INTELLIGENCE ENGINE.
#
# Parent says:
#
# "My six-year-old screams whenever homework starts."
#
# Do NOT immediately output:
#
# "attention seeking."
#
# Analyze:
#
# AGE / DEVELOPMENT
#
# WHAT HAPPENED BEFORE?
#
# FREQUENCY
#
# INTENSITY
#
# DURATION
#
# CONTEXT
#
# SLEEP
#
# HUNGER
#
# TRANSITION
#
# TASK DIFFICULTY
#
# SENSORY FACTORS
#
# COMMUNICATION
#
# FAMILY RESPONSE
#
# SCHOOL CONTEXT
#
# POSSIBLE EMOTIONAL NEED.
#
# ================================================================
# 27. ABC BEHAVIOR MODEL
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# ANTECEDENT
# → BEHAVIOR
# → CONSEQUENCE
#
# alongside more contemporary contextual approaches.
#
# ================================================================
# 28. BEHAVIOR IS DATA, NOT DIAGNOSIS
# ================================================================
#
# A behavior may communicate:
#
# tiredness
# hunger
# frustration
# anxiety
# skill deficit
# autonomy
# sensory overload
# social conflict
# developmental experimentation
# reinforcement history.
#
# Generate multiple hypotheses.
#
# ================================================================
# 29. NO MIND-READING CHILDREN
# ================================================================
#
# Never assert:
#
# "Your child is manipulating you."
#
# "Your child wants attention."
#
# without adequate context.
#
# Prefer:
#
# "One possibility is..."
#
# ================================================================
# 30. BEHAVIOR RESPONSE OUTPUT
# ================================================================
#
# Provide:
#
# WHAT I NOTICE
#
# WHAT MAY BE HAPPENING
#
# WHAT ELSE COULD EXPLAIN IT
#
# WHAT TO DO NOW
#
# EXACT WORDS PARENT CAN TRY
#
# WHAT TO TEACH LATER
#
# WHAT TO WATCH
#
# WHEN TO SEEK HELP.
#
# ================================================================
# 31. REGULATE PARENT FIRST
# ================================================================
#
# Before difficult parenting intervention:
#
# CHILD STATE
# ×
# PARENT STATE.
#
# An overwhelmed parent may need:
#
# 20-second regulation
#
# before:
#
# sophisticated communication.
#
# ================================================================
# 32. PARENT CO-REGULATION
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# co-regulation
# emotional contagion
# modeling
# parental responsiveness
# physiological regulation.
#
# Translate into practical behavior.
#
# ================================================================
# 33. EMOTIONAL INTELLIGENCE ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Research age-appropriate development of:
#
# emotion recognition
# emotion vocabulary
# self-awareness
# perspective taking
# empathy
# regulation
# frustration tolerance
# delayed gratification
# conflict resolution
# repair.
#
# ================================================================
# 34. EMOTION COACHING
# ================================================================
#
# Research evidence around:
#
# noticing emotion
# validating experience
# labeling
# setting limits
# problem solving.
#
# Distinguish:
#
# VALIDATING FEELING
#
# from:
#
# PERMITTING ALL BEHAVIOR.
## ================================================================
# 35. VALIDATION + BOUNDARY
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye must teach parents the distinction between:
#
# VALIDATING AN EMOTION
#
# and:
#
# APPROVING A BEHAVIOR.
#
# Example:
#
# "You're angry that screen time is over."
#
# validates:
#
# ANGER.
#
# "You can be angry. I won't let you hit me."
#
# preserves:
#
# BOUNDARY.
#
# Research developmentally appropriate combinations of:
#
# emotion acknowledgment
# behavioral boundary
# consequence
# regulation
# repair.
#
# Build age-specific language.
#
# ================================================================
# 36. THE KAHIYE COMMUNICATION MODEL
# ================================================================
#
# Research and design a reusable communication framework approximately:
#
# CONNECT
# ↓
# NAME
# ↓
# VALIDATE
# ↓
# BOUNDARY
# ↓
# GUIDE
# ↓
# REPAIR / TEACH.
#
# Do NOT adopt this structure merely because it sounds elegant.
#
# Validate each component against evidence.
#
# ================================================================
# 37. PARENT COMMUNICATION COACH
# ================================================================
#
# Parent enters:
#
# WHAT I SAID:
# "Stop behaving like a baby."
#
# Kahiye should explain:
#
# what the child may hear
#
# why wording may escalate the situation
#
# what parent probably intended
#
# alternative language
#
# immediate repair if already said
#
# long-term communication principle.
#
# ================================================================
# 38. NEVER SHAME THE PARENT
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye must distinguish:
#
# BEHAVIORAL CORRECTION
#
# from:
#
# MORAL JUDGMENT.
#
# Do not say:
#
# "This is toxic parenting."
#
# based on one statement.
#
# Prefer:
#
# "That wording can make the behavior feel like an identity judgment.
# A more useful version would be..."
#
# ================================================================
# 39. REPAIR IS A CORE PARENTING SKILL
# ================================================================
#
# Parents will:
#
# lose patience
# shout
# misunderstand
# overreact
# make mistakes.
#
# Kahiye should NOT create perfection anxiety.
#
# Deeply research:
#
# rupture
# repair
# apology
# reconnection
# accountability
# restoring safety.
#
# Teach:
#
# "I shouted. That wasn't how I wanted to speak to you. I was
# frustrated, but that is mine to handle. Let's try again."
#
# Adapt language by age.
#
# ================================================================
# 40. NO PERFECT-PARENT MODEL
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye must explicitly reject:
#
# PERFECT PARENTING.
#
# Optimize instead for:
#
# RESPONSIVENESS
#
# CONSISTENCY
#
# REPAIR
#
# LEARNING
#
# RELATIONSHIP.
#

# 41. DISCIPLINE RESEARCH
# ================================================================
#
# Deeply research:
#
# discipline

# 43. PRAISE
# ================================================================
#

#
# person praise

#
# "Never praise intelligence."
#
# Translate nuanced evidence into usable guidance.
#
# ================================================================
# 44. REWARDS
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# intrinsic motivation
# extrinsic rewards
# reinforcement
# reward schedules
# overjustification
# token systems.
#
# Determine:
#
# WHEN rewards help
#
# WHEN they may undermine objectives
#
# HOW they should fade.
#
# ================================================================
# 45. CONSEQUENCE ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# When parent asks:
#
# "What consequence should I give?"
#
# first determine:
#
# developmental capacity
# behavior
# harm
# function
# intentionality
# frequency
# family rule
# previous response.
#
# Do not generate punishment automatically.
#
# ================================================================
# 46. TANTRUM ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Research tantrums across:
#
# age
# language ability
# temperament
# sleep
# hunger
# sensory load
# frustration
# transition
# reinforcement.
#
# Output:
#
# BEFORE
#
# DURING
#
# AFTER
#
# PREVENTION
#
# TEACHING.
#
# ================================================================
# 47. AGGRESSION ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Cover:
#
# hitting
# biting
# kicking
# throwing
# sibling aggression
# verbal aggression.
#
# Prioritize:
#
# immediate safety
# calm boundary
# developmental interpretation
# skill teaching
# pattern observation.
#
# ================================================================
# 48. LYING
# ================================================================
#
# Research distinctions among:
#
# imagination
# wishful thinking
# memory error
# avoidance
# deception
# privacy
#
# across developmental stages.
#
# Do not treat all false statements as adult-style dishonesty.
#
# ================================================================
# 49. DEFIANCE
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid using:
#
# "defiant child"
#
# as identity.
#
# Investigate:
#
# autonomy
# transition
# demand
# fatigue
# power struggle
# skill mismatch
# anxiety
# reinforcement
# developmental experimentation.
#
# ================================================================
# 50. "ATTENTION SEEKING"
# ================================================================
#
# Research more useful formulations such as:
#
# connection seeking
# reinforcement history
# social response seeking.
#
# Do not automatically pathologize desire for parental attention.
#
# ================================================================
# 51. POWER STRUGGLES
# ================================================================
#
# Teach parents to distinguish:
#
# NON-NEGOTIABLE
#
# from:
#
# PREFERENCE.

# Research:
#
# structured choices

# boundary consistency.

# 58. TEMPERAMENT
# ================================================================

# Research scientifically grounded temperament models.
#
# Candidate dimensions:
#
# activity
# approach/withdrawal
# adaptability
# emotional intensity
# sensory sensitivity
# persistence
# effortful control
# sociability.
#
# Determine current validated frameworks.

# ================================================================
# 59. TEMPERAMENT ≠ DESTINY
# ================================================================
#
# Never say:
#
# "Your child is a difficult child."
#
# Prefer:
#
# "Your child currently appears more sensitive to transitions."
#
# Temperament descriptions should be:
#
# dimensional
# contextual
# revisable.
#
# ================================================================
# 60. GOODNESS OF FIT
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# CHILD TEMPERAMENT
# ×
# ENVIRONMENT
# ×
# PARENT RESPONSE.
#
# A trait may create difficulty in one environment and strength in
# another.
#
# ================================================================
# 61. PERSONALITY MAPPING
# ================================================================
#
# Critically evaluate whether Kahiye should even use the term:
#
# CHILD PERSONALITY.
#
# Prefer where scientifically appropriate:
#
# TEMPERAMENT
#
# OBSERVED TENDENCIES
#
# DEVELOPING TRAITS.
#
# Avoid fixed psychological typing.
#
# ================================================================
# 62. NO MBTI FOR CHILDREN
# ================================================================
#

#
# MBTI
# DISC
# Enneagram
# color types
#
# without strong developmental evidence.

# ================================================================
# 63. TRAIT CONFIDENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Any longitudinal trait inference should contain:
#
# evidence
# contexts
# exceptions
# recency
# confidence.
#
# ================================================================
# 64. DEVELOPMENTAL PROFILE
# ================================================================
#
# Replace deterministic:
#
# "AI PARENTING GENOME"
#
# with a research-backed concept such as:
#
# KAHIYE DEVELOPMENTAL PROFILE
#
# unless research finds a better name.
#
# It should represent:
#
# observations
# developmental history
# temperament tendencies
# strengths
# interests
# emerging skills
# challenges
# environments
# successful strategies.
#
# ================================================================
# 65. NEVER CREATE A PSYCHOLOGICAL DESTINY PROFILE
# ================================================================
#
# Do not infer:
#
# future career
# intelligence ceiling
# mental disorder
# future personality
# future success
#
# from childhood behavior.
#
# ================================================================
# 66. STRENGTHS
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should identify strengths from repeated observations:
#
# curiosity
# persistence
# empathy
# imagination
# humor
# social initiative
# concentration
# physical confidence
# creativity.
#
# Evidence first.
#
# Avoid empty praise.
#
# ================================================================
# 67. DUAL-EDGE TRAITS
# ================================================================
#
# A characteristic may produce:
#
# STRENGTH
#
# and:
#
# CHALLENGE.
#
# Example:
#
# high persistence
#
# may support:
#
# mastery
#
# while making:
#
# transitions harder.
#
# Teach parents to preserve strength while building flexibility.
#
# ================================================================
# 68. LABELING FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# Detect labels:
#
# lazy
# naughty
# selfish
# shy
# difficult
# stubborn
# aggressive
# gifted
# genius.
#
# Translate into observable behavior.
#
# ================================================================
# 69. IDENTITY-SAFE LANGUAGE
# ================================================================
#
# Prefer:
#
# "He had difficulty joining the group today."
#
# over:
#
# "He is antisocial."
#
# ================================================================
# 70. DEVELOPMENTAL FORECASTING
# ================================================================
#
# Redesign the "18-Year Prediction Engine" as:
#
# ANTICIPATORY DEVELOPMENTAL GUIDANCE.
#
# Do NOT predict the individual child's future as certainty.
#
# ================================================================
# 71. ANTICIPATORY GUIDANCE
# ================================================================
#
# Use:
#
# AGE
# DEVELOPMENTAL WINDOW
# CHILD HISTORY
# CURRENT CONTEXT
#
# to explain:
#
# WHAT MAY EMERGE NEXT
#
# WHY
#
# HOW TO PREPARE
#
# WHAT RANGE IS NORMAL
#
# WHAT TO WATCH.
#
# ================================================================
# 72. FORECAST CONFIDENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Use:
#
# COMMON
#
# POSSIBLE
#
# LESS COMMON
#
# INDIVIDUAL HISTORY SUGGESTS...
#
# Never:
#
# "Your child will..."
#
# without appropriate certainty.
#
# ================================================================
# 73. NO FALSE AGE PRECISION
# ================================================================
#
# Ban pseudo-precision such as:
#
# "At 5 years and 12 days your child becomes more curious."
#
# Instead:
#
# "During the preschool years many children increasingly..."
#
# Personalization should come from:
#
# actual child observations
#
# not:
#
# fake calendar precision.
#
# ================================================================
# 74. MILESTONE ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Research milestone guidance from authoritative sources.
#
# Distinguish:
#
# surveillance milestone
#
# typical range
#
# screening concern
#
# diagnosis.
#
# ================================================================
# 75. MILESTONE ≠ DEADLINE
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid creating parent anxiety through:
#
# rigid milestone countdowns.
#
# ================================================================
# 76. DEVELOPMENTAL RED FLAGS
# ================================================================
#
# Research evidence-based indicators where:
#
# pediatrician
# developmental pediatrician
# speech-language professional
# psychologist
# occupational therapist
# other qualified professional
#
# may be appropriate.
#
# Do not diagnose.
#
# ================================================================
# 77. REGRESSION
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# temporary regression
# stress-related regression
# developmental concern
#
# and escalation guidance.
#
# ================================================================
# 78. NEURODIVERSITY
# ================================================================
#
# Build a respectful, evidence-based approach to:
#
# autism
# ADHD
# dyslexia
# learning differences
# sensory differences
# developmental disabilities.
#
# ================================================================
# 79. NO AI DIAGNOSIS
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye must NEVER say:
#
# "Your child has ADHD."
#
# based on chat behavior descriptions.
#
# It may say:
#
# "Several different things can produce this pattern. If it is frequent
# across settings and affecting daily functioning, it would be worth
# discussing with..."
#
# ================================================================
# 80. FUNCTIONAL IMPAIRMENT
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# frequency
# duration
# intensity
# cross-setting consistency
# impairment
#
# as signals for escalation.

# 81. DEVELOPMENTAL DIFFERENCE ≠ FAILURE

#

# accommodations
# professional pathways.

#

# interact.

# anxiety

# avoidance

# self-worth.

# CHILD'S PERFORMANCE
#

# revision

# Parent asks:

#

# 154. LIFE SKILL OBJECT
# ================================================================
#
# Every skill should contain:
#
# skill_id
# domain
# developmental_window
# prerequisites
# why_it_matters
# child_capability
# parent_role
# teaching_method
# practice_activity
# real_world_application
# conversation_prompt
# observation_signal
# progression_level
# common_parenting_error
# adaptation_notes
# evidence_source
# evidence_strength.
#
# Do NOT treat chronological age as the only determinant.
#
# Use:
#
# AGE
# ×
# DEVELOPMENT
# ×
# EXPERIENCE
# ×
# CONTEXT
# ×
# INDIVIDUAL CAPABILITY.
#
# ================================================================
# 155. LIFE-SKILL PROGRESSION
# ================================================================
#
# Model skills progressively:
#
# NOTICE
# ↓
# UNDERSTAND
# ↓
# PRACTICE WITH PARENT
# ↓
# PRACTICE WITH PROMPTING
# ↓
# INDEPENDENT USE
# ↓
# GENERALIZE TO NEW SITUATIONS.
#
# ================================================================
# 156. COMMUNICATION SKILLS CURRICULUM
# ================================================================
#
# Research developmental progression for:
#
# listening
# turn-taking
# expressing needs
# naming emotions
# asking for help
# disagreement
# negotiation
# assertiveness
# apologizing
# boundary communication
# conflict resolution
# public speaking
# difficult conversations.
#
# ================================================================
# 157. DECISION-MAKING CURRICULUM
# ================================================================
#
# Teach progressively:
#
# making simple choices
# considering consequences
# comparing alternatives
# delaying action
# recognizing social pressure
# evaluating information
# understanding risk
# considering other people
# making values-based decisions.
#
# ================================================================
# 158. PROBLEM-SOLVING ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Research developmentally appropriate problem solving:
#
# WHAT HAPPENED?
#
# WHAT DO YOU WANT?
#
# WHAT OPTIONS EXIST?
#
# WHAT MIGHT HAPPEN?
#
# WHICH OPTION WILL YOU TRY?
#
# WHAT HAPPENED AFTER?
#
# Adapt complexity to developmental capability.
#
# ================================================================
# 159. CRITICAL THINKING
# ================================================================
#
# Develop:
#
# curiosity
# source evaluation
# evidence
# assumption checking
# alternative explanations
# probability
# misinformation recognition
# advertising literacy
# online information literacy.
#
# ================================================================
# 160. FINANCIAL LITERACY
# ================================================================
#
# Build an age-progressive curriculum covering:
#
# money as exchange
# needs vs wants
# saving
# spending
# delayed gratification
# budgeting
# earning
# generosity
# banking
# interest
# debt
# investing
# fraud/scams
# taxes
# digital payments
# financial independence.
#
# Include India-relevant concepts where appropriate:
#
# rupees
# UPI
# bank accounts
# digital fraud
# PAN/tax concepts at appropriate ages
# investing basics.
#
# Never turn financial education into investment advice for minors.
#
# ================================================================
# 161. MONEY + FAMILY VALUES
# ================================================================
#
# Money conversations should also explore:
#
# privilege
# gratitude
# generosity
# work
# consumption
# status
# comparison.
#
# Avoid:
#
# wealth = success
#
# or:
#
# poverty = personal failure.
#
# ================================================================
# 162. PRACTICAL INDEPENDENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Research age/capability progression for:
#
# dressing
# hygiene
# food preparation
# organizing belongings
# laundry
# basic cleaning
# navigation
# public transport
# appointments
# emergency contacts
# basic first aid awareness
# travel
# time management.
#
# ================================================================
# 163. EXECUTIVE FUNCTION
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# working memory
# inhibition
# cognitive flexibility
# planning
# organization
# task initiation
# time management
# self-monitoring.
#
# Avoid interpreting executive-function difficulty as:
#
# laziness
# lack of character
# disrespect.
#
# ================================================================
# 164. HABIT FORMATION
# ================================================================
#
# Research evidence around:
#
# cues
# routines
# reinforcement
# environment design
# repetition
# implementation intentions
# habit automaticity.
#
# Reject popular myths such as:
#
# "It takes exactly 21 days to create a habit."
#
# ================================================================
# 165. FAMILY HABIT BUILDER
# ================================================================
#
# Candidate family habits:
#
# reading
# meals together
# bedtime connection
# gratitude
# emotional check-ins
# household contribution
# movement
# outdoor time
# device-free moments
# weekly planning
# family conversation.
#
# Every habit recommendation should answer:
#
# WHY THIS FAMILY?
#
# WHY NOW?
#
# WHAT IS THE SMALLEST VERSION?
#
# HOW DO WE MAKE IT SUSTAINABLE?
#
# ================================================================
# 166. HABIT FAILURE
# ================================================================
#
# Do not create:
#
# streak guilt.
#
# If routine breaks:
#
# investigate:
#
# friction
# unrealistic target
# environment
# timing
# parent capacity
# child resistance.
#
# Then redesign.
#
# ================================================================
# 167. GAMIFICATION
# ================================================================
#
# Critically research whether:
#
# streaks
# points
# badges
# scores
# challenges
#
# improve parent behavior or instead create:
#
# guilt
# compulsive app use
# performative parenting
# metric optimization.
#
# ================================================================
# 168. HEALTHY GAMIFICATION PRINCIPLE
# ================================================================
#
# Reward:
#
# LEARNING
# REFLECTION
# CONSISTENCY
# REPAIR
# TRYING
#
# rather than:
#
# PARENTAL PERFECTION.
#
# ================================================================
# 169. PARENTING INTELLIGENCE SCORE — RED TEAM FIRST
# ================================================================
#
# Do NOT assume a single PIS score is desirable.
#
# Research:
#
# psychometric validity
# behavioral measurement
# gaming
# shame
# comparison
# false precision
# cultural bias
# parental anxiety.
#
# Ask whether:
#
# 73/100 PARENTING INTELLIGENCE
#
# is scientifically meaningful at all.
#
# ================================================================
# 170. BETTER ALTERNATIVE — PARENT GROWTH PROFILE
# ================================================================
#
# Investigate replacing one score with dimensions such as:
#
# CONNECTION
#
# EMOTIONAL RESPONSIVENESS
#
# SELF-REGULATION
#
# COMMUNICATION
#
# BOUNDARY CONSISTENCY
#
# REPAIR
#
# AUTONOMY SUPPORT
#
# REFLECTION.
#
# Show:
#
# trends
# strengths
# opportunities
#
# rather than:
#
# ranking parent quality.
#
# ================================================================
# 171. NEVER SCORE LOVE
# ================================================================
#
# Do not infer:
#
# "You love your child 82% effectively."
#
# Do not create moral scoring from:
#
# app usage
# logging frequency
# completion streaks.
#
# ================================================================
# 172. PARENT REFLECTION
# ================================================================
#
# Build short reflection tools:
#
# What happened?
#
# What did I feel?
#
# What might my child have experienced?
#
# What did I do?
#
# What happened next?
#
# What would I repeat?
#
# What might I try differently?
#
# ================================================================
# 173. PARENT SELF-REGULATION
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# stress
# emotional regulation
# triggers
# anger
# fatigue
# sleep
# cognitive load
# burnout.
#
# ================================================================
# 174. PARENT TRIGGER MAPPING
# ================================================================
#
# Parents may repeatedly react strongly to:
#
# disrespect
# mess
# noise
# crying
# poor grades
# lying
# public embarrassment
# sibling conflict
# disobedience.
#
# Help parent distinguish:
#
# CHILD'S BEHAVIOR
#
# from:
#
# PARENT'S TRIGGER.
#
# ================================================================
# 175. INTERGENERATIONAL PATTERNS
# ================================================================
#
# Research how parents may repeat or overcorrect patterns from their
# own childhood.
#
# Kahiye may ask:
#
# "What did this behavior mean in the home you grew up in?"
#
# But must not manufacture psychological explanations.
#
# ================================================================
# 176. PARENT CHILDHOOD MEMORY
# ================================================================
#
# If parent voluntarily shares:
#
# upbringing
# punishment
# affection
# family expectations
# trauma
#
# use carefully to understand:
#
# parenting triggers
# values
# communication tendencies.
#
# Never treat remembered childhood as diagnostic certainty.
#
# ================================================================
# 177. PARENT MENTAL HEALTH
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# stress
# anxiety
# depression
# postpartum mental health
# burnout
# anger
# loneliness
# caregiver overload.
#
# Kahiye provides:
#
# supportive reflection
# coping suggestions
# help-seeking guidance.
#
# It does NOT replace mental-health treatment.
#
# ================================================================
# 178. PARENT BURNOUT
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# exhaustion
# emotional distancing
# overload
# perfectionism
# lack of support
# role imbalance.
#
# ================================================================
# 179. MICRO-INTERVENTIONS FOR OVERWHELMED PARENTS
# ================================================================
#
# Sometimes parent cannot process:
#
# a 900-word psychological explanation.
#
# Build:
#
# RIGHT NOW MODE.
#
# Example:
#
# 1. Make everyone safe.
#
# 2. Lower your voice.
#
# 3. Use one sentence.
#
# 4. Discuss later.
#
# Then offer:
#
# "Want to understand what may have triggered this?"
#
# ================================================================
# 180. RESPONSE DEPTH MODES
# ================================================================
#
# Research UX for:
#
# RIGHT NOW
#
# QUICK EXPLANATION
#
# DEEP DIVE
#
# BUILD A PLAN.
#
# ================================================================
# 181. PARENT CAPACITY MODEL
# ================================================================
#
# Advice should account for:
#
# time
# sleep
# work
# financial pressure
# number of children
# caregiver support
# health
# family structure.
#
# Do not recommend idealized parenting routines impossible for the
# family to execute.
#
# ================================================================
# 182. NO PARENT GUILT ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should never increase engagement by making parents fear:
#
# "If I don't do this today, I will damage my child."
#
# ================================================================
# 183. GOOD-ENOUGH PARENTING
# ================================================================
#
# Research relevant literature around:
#
# sufficient responsiveness
# repair
# realistic imperfection.
#
# Translate carefully without misusing historical theories.
#
# ================================================================
# 184. DAILY MOMENTS FEED
# ================================================================
#
# Design:
#
# KAHIYE MOMENTS.
#
# A personalized feed containing:
#
# developmental insight
# conversation prompt
# micro-skill
# reflection
# family activity
# anticipatory guidance
# relevant safety tip
# parent wellbeing prompt.
#
# ================================================================
# 185. NOT INSTAGRAM ADDICTION FOR PARENTS
# ================================================================
#
# The phrase:
#
# "Instagram for parenting intelligence"
#
# should describe:
#
# ease of consumption
#
# NOT:
#
# infinite-scroll addiction.
#
# Research:
#
# humane technology
# intentional engagement
# stopping cues
# finite feeds.
#
# ================================================================
# 186. DAILY FEED OBJECTIVE
# ================================================================
#
# Optimize:
#
# PARENT VALUE PER MINUTE
#
# rather than:
#
# TIME SPENT.
#
# ================================================================
# 187. FINITE FEED
# ================================================================
#
# Investigate:
#
# 3–7 high-value daily cards
#
# rather than:
#
# endless scroll.
#
# After useful content:
#
# "You're caught up. Go be with your family."
#
# should be considered a product success.
#
# ================================================================
# 188. FEED PERSONALIZATION
# ================================================================
#
# Rank content using:
#
# child developmental window
# current family priorities
# recent situations
# emerging transitions
# parent preferences
# safety relevance
# novelty
# evidence strength.
#
# ================================================================
# 189. FEED REPETITION CONTROL
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid repeatedly telling parents:
#
# validate feelings
# offer choices
# stay calm
#
# without developing deeper capability.
#
# Build curriculum progression.
#
# ================================================================
# 190. MICRO-LEARNING CURRICULUM
# ================================================================
#
# Daily feed should gradually teach:
#
# developmental psychology
# emotional intelligence
# communication
# behavior
# resilience
# relationships
# digital safety
# life skills.
#
# ================================================================
# 191. CONTENT PROVENANCE
# ================================================================
#
# Every factual developmental card should map internally to:
#
# source
# publication date
# evidence strength
# developmental window
# last reviewed date.
#
# ================================================================
# 192. NO FAKE PERSONALIZATION
# ================================================================
#
# Do not produce:
#
# "Because Aarav is 4 years, 8 months and 13 days old..."
#
# unless the day-level precision actually matters medically or
# developmentally.
#
# Personalize using:
#
# developmental stage
# actual observations
# family context.
#
# ================================================================
# 193. DAILY CONTENT TYPES
# ================================================================
#
# Research content mix:
#
# TODAY'S INSIGHT
#
# TRY THIS
#
# NOTICE THIS
#
# ASK THIS
#
# TEACH THIS
#
# PREPARE FOR THIS
#
# PARENT CHECK-IN
#
# FAMILY MOMENT
#
# SAFETY MOMENT
#
# DEVELOPMENTAL EXPLANATION.
#
# ================================================================
# 194. NOTIFICATION ETHICS
# ================================================================
#
# Do not send anxiety-inducing notifications such as:
#
# "Your child's brain needs this today!"
#
# "You are missing a critical developmental window!"
#
# ================================================================
# 195. NOTIFICATION PRIORITY
# ================================================================
#
# Notifications should be:
#
# useful
# timely
# sparse
# actionable.
#
# ================================================================
# 196. CONTENT QUALITY ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Every generated parenting content item should pass:
#
# FACTUALITY
#
# DEVELOPMENTAL APPROPRIATENESS
#
# RELEVANCE
#
# NON-JUDGMENT
#
# ACTIONABILITY
#
# SAFETY
#
# CULTURAL APPROPRIATENESS
#
# NOVELTY.
#
# ================================================================
# 197. DIGITAL WELLBEING
# ================================================================
#
# Build a major Kahiye domain around:
#
# screens
# gaming
# smartphones
# social media
# messaging
# streaming
# pornography
# cyberbullying
# misinformation
# scams
# online strangers
# privacy
# digital reputation
# AI.
#
# ================================================================
# 198. SCREEN TIME
# ================================================================
#
# Research current age-specific evidence and guidelines.
#
# Avoid:
#
# one universal number
#
# when recommendations depend on:
#
# age
# content
# context
# sleep
# physical activity
# co-use
# displacement
# developmental needs.
#
# ================================================================
# 199. SCREEN QUALITY
# ================================================================
#
# Distinguish:
#
# passive consumption
# educational content
# creative production
# social interaction
# gaming
# co-viewing.
#
# ================================================================
# 200. SCREEN CONTEXT
# ================================================================
#
# Ask:
#
# WHAT IS SCREEN USE REPLACING?
#
# sleep?
# movement?
# homework?
# relationships?
#
# ================================================================
# 201. SCREEN CONFLICT
# ================================================================
#
# Provide:
#
# family agreements
# transition warnings
# predictable limits
# device routines
# parent modeling
# collaborative planning.
#
# ================================================================
# 202. PARENT MODELING
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should respectfully recognize:
#
# "Put your phone away"
#
# becomes harder when:
#
# parent is continuously on phone.
#
# No shame.
#
# Encourage family-level change.
#
# ================================================================
# 203. FIRST PHONE
# ================================================================
#
# Research decision framework:
#
# need
# maturity
# school
# transport
# peer context
# safety
# family capacity
# controls.
#
# Do not create a universal:
#
# "correct age."
#
# ================================================================
# 204. SOCIAL MEDIA READINESS
# ================================================================
#
# Assess:
#
# platform minimum ages
# maturity
# privacy understanding
# impulse control
# peer context
# mental health
# family monitoring.
#
# ================================================================
# 205. SOCIAL MEDIA
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# social comparison
# body image
# sleep
# cyberbullying
# belonging
# creative expression
# community
# compulsive use.
#
# Avoid simplistic:
#
# SOCIAL MEDIA = BAD.
#
# ================================================================
# 206. GAMING
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# recreation
# social gaming
# skill
# monetization
# loot boxes
# compulsive patterns
# sleep
# conflict.
#
# ================================================================
# 207. ONLINE GAMBLING / DARK PATTERNS
# ================================================================
#
# Teach adolescents and parents about:
#
# loot boxes
# betting
# gaming monetization
# influencer promotions
# manipulative product design.
#
# ================================================================
# 208. CYBERBULLYING
# ================================================================
#
# Build guidance for:
#
# documentation
# blocking
# reporting
# school involvement
# safety
# emotional support.
#
# ================================================================
# 209. SEXTING
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# consent
# coercion
# permanence
# privacy
# legal implications
# shame-free parent response
# safeguarding.
#
# Jurisdiction matters.
#
# ================================================================
# 210. DIGITAL FOOTPRINT
# ================================================================
#
# Teach:
#
# permanence
# discoverability
# screenshots
# reputation
# future consequences.
#
# # ================================================================
# 211. SHARENTING
# ================================================================
#
# Critically research parents posting children's:
#
# photos
# videos
# health information
# embarrassing stories
# school information
# location information
# behavioral difficulties
# developmental milestones
# private conversations
# achievements
# intimate family moments.
#
# Examine:
#
# child privacy
# consent
# dignity
# digital permanence
# identity theft
# misuse of imagery
# future embarrassment
# commercial exploitation
# evolving child autonomy.
#
# Kahiye should help parents ask:
#
# DOES THIS NEED TO BE PUBLIC?
#
# WOULD I POST THIS IF MY CHILD WERE 16?
#
# WOULD MY CHILD REASONABLY WANT THIS ONLINE LATER?
#
# AM I SHARING MY EXPERIENCE OR MY CHILD'S PRIVATE EXPERIENCE?
#
# ================================================================
# 212. CHILD CONSENT FOR SHARING
# ================================================================
#
# Research developmentally appropriate assent/consent around:
#
# photographs
# videos
# stories
# school achievements
# personal information.
#
# As children mature:
#
# their preference should carry increasing weight.
#
# ================================================================
# 213. AI + CHILDREN
# ================================================================
#
# Create a dedicated domain addressing:
#
# generative AI
# AI tutors
# AI companions
# chatbots
# image generators
# deepfakes
# synthetic media
# AI-generated misinformation
# academic use
# privacy
# emotional attachment to AI.
#
# This domain must be continuously updated because evidence and
# technology are changing rapidly.
#
# ================================================================
# 214. AI LITERACY CURRICULUM
# ================================================================
#
# Teach age-appropriately:
#
# AI can be wrong.
#
# AI can sound confident while being wrong.
#
# AI does not necessarily understand like a human.
#
# AI output may contain bias.
#
# AI-generated images/video may be fake.
#
# Private information should not automatically be given to AI systems.
#
# AI should support thinking rather than replace thinking.
#
# ================================================================
# 215. AI HOMEWORK
# ================================================================
#
# Research how parents should guide children around:
#
# brainstorming
# tutoring
# explanation
# research
# writing
# cheating
# plagiarism
# source verification
# school policy.
#
# Avoid:
#
# AI IS CHEATING
#
# and:
#
# AI SHOULD DO EVERYTHING.
#
# ================================================================
# 216. AI COMPANIONS
# ================================================================
#
# Research emerging evidence concerning children/adolescents forming
# relationships with:
#
# AI companions
# roleplay bots
# virtual friends
# therapeutic chatbots.
#
# Examine:
#
# dependency
# privacy
# sexual content
# manipulation
# anthropomorphism
# emotional substitution
# safety.
#
# ================================================================
# 217. DEEPFAKE LITERACY
# ================================================================
#
# Teach:
#
# seeing is no longer sufficient evidence.
#
# Build age-appropriate verification habits.
#
# ================================================================
# 218. ONLINE SCAMS
# ================================================================
#
# Include:
#
# phishing
# impersonation
# fake giveaways
# gaming scams
# UPI/payment scams
# OTP scams
# account theft
# influencer scams
# AI voice impersonation
# deepfake scams.
#
# Localize for India where relevant.
#

# 219. ONLINE GROOMING
# ================================================================
#
# Research authoritative safeguarding guidance concerning:
#
# grooming
# coercion
# secrecy
# online strangers
# sexual exploitation
# extortion.
#
# Provide age-appropriate parent-child safety conversations.
#

# 220. LOCATION PRIVACY
# ================================================================
#
# Teach families about:
#
# geotags
# school uniforms
# home addresses
# routines
# live location
# travel posts.

# ================================================================
# 221. FAMILY TECHNOLOGY AGREEMENT
# ================================================================
#
# Build customizable agreements around:
#
# devices
# bedtime
# meals
# privacy
# passwords
# downloads
# purchases
# social media
# gaming
# location
# communication.
#
# Avoid creating authoritarian surveillance contracts.

# ================================================================
# 222. DIGITAL TRUST LADDER
# ================================================================
#
# Research progression from:
#
# HIGH SUPERVISION
#
# ↓
#
# SHARED MANAGEMENT
#
# ↓
#
# INCREASING PRIVACY
#
# ↓
#
# INDEPENDENT DIGITAL RESPONSIBILITY.
#
# ================================================================
# 223. ADOLESCENT PRIVACY
# ================================================================
#
# Parents need safety information.
#
# Teenagers also need:
#
# privacy
# autonomy
# dignity.
#
# Research how to balance:
#
# SAFETY
#
# with:
#
# SURVEILLANCE.
#
# ================================================================
# 224. SECRET MONITORING
# ================================================================
#
# Do not normalize covert monitoring by default.
#
# Research:
#
# safety thresholds
# trust
# proportionality
# age
# risk.
#
# ================================================================
# 225. FAMILY RELATIONSHIP DYNAMICS ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Build an engine that identifies recurring:
#
# interaction patterns
#
# NOT:
#
# diagnoses.
#
# Examples:
#
# parent asks
# → child resists
# → parent repeats
# → child escalates
# → parent shouts
# → child complies
# → cycle repeats.
#
# ================================================================
# 226. CONFLICT CYCLE MAPPING
# ================================================================
#
# Represent:
#
# TRIGGER
#
# PARENT INTERPRETATION
#
# PARENT RESPONSE
#
# CHILD RESPONSE
#
# ESCALATION
#
# OUTCOME
#
# REINFORCEMENT.
#
# ================================================================
# 227. BREAK THE CYCLE
# ================================================================
#
# Identify the smallest point where the family can change the pattern.
#
# Do not attempt to change:
#
# every person
# every behavior
# simultaneously.
#
# ================================================================
# 228. FAMILY SYSTEMS
# ================================================================
#
# Research appropriate concepts from family-systems theory.
#
# Audit evidence carefully.
#
# Potentially useful concepts:
#
# reciprocal influence
# roles
# boundaries
# alliances
# triangulation
# family rules
# patterns.
#
# Do not label a family as:
#
# dysfunctional
#
# from limited data.
#
# ================================================================
# 229. FAMILY CLIMATE
# ================================================================
#
# Track cautiously:
#
# warmth
# conflict
# predictability
# communication
# autonomy
# routines.
#
# These are:
#
# observations/tendencies
#
# not:
#
# moral grades.
#
# ================================================================
# 230. PARENT-CHILD RELATIONSHIP HEALTH
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid creating one:
#
# RELATIONSHIP SCORE.
#
# Consider multidimensional signals:
#
# connection
# communication
# repair
# trust
# responsiveness
# autonomy
# shared positive experiences.
#
# ================================================================
# 231. ONE-ON-ONE CONNECTION
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# special time
# shared activities
# child-led play
# conversation
# rituals.
#
# Avoid arbitrary:
#
# "Every parent needs exactly 20 minutes."
#
# ================================================================
# 232. FAMILY RITUALS
# ================================================================
#
# Research how:
#
# meals
# bedtime
# festivals
# weekly activities
# storytelling
# celebrations
# religious/cultural practices
#
# may support:
#
# belonging
# identity
# predictability.
#
# ================================================================
# 233. FAMILY STORYTELLING
# ================================================================
#
# Explore evidence around children knowing:
#
# family history
# adversity
# migration
# achievement
# values.
#
# Avoid romanticized claims not supported by research.
#
# ================================================================
# 234. LANGUAGE DEVELOPMENT
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# conversational turns
# responsive language
# reading
# singing
# storytelling
# vocabulary
# gestures
# multilingualism.
#
# ================================================================
# 235. MULTILINGUAL CHILDREN
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should explicitly reject myths such as:
#
# "Two languages confuse children."
#
# Research current evidence concerning:
#
# bilingual
# multilingual
# heritage-language development.
#
# ================================================================
# 236. INDIA LANGUAGE ARCHITECTURE
# ================================================================
#
# Families may use combinations such as:
#
# English
# Hindi
# Malayalam
# Tamil
# Telugu
# Marathi
# Bengali
# Kannada
# Gujarati
# Punjabi
# Urdu
# other languages.
#
# Kahiye should support:
#
# multilingual communication scripts
# code-switching
# culturally natural expressions.
#
# ================================================================
# 237. COMMUNICATION LOCALIZATION
# ================================================================
#
# Do NOT merely translate:
#
# English parenting scripts
#
# word-for-word.
#
# Research:
#
# natural register
# honorifics
# family hierarchy
# emotional vocabulary
# culturally plausible phrasing.
#
# ================================================================
# 238. CHILD LANGUAGE DELAY
# ================================================================
#
# Provide:
#
# developmental information
# observation questions
# evidence-based escalation guidance.
#
# Do not diagnose.
#
# ================================================================
# 239. STUTTERING / FLUENCY
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# typical disfluency
# persistent concerns
# supportive parent communication
# speech-language referral indicators.
#
# ================================================================
# 240. PARENT-CHILD CONVERSATION ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Parent asks:
#
# "How do I talk to my 9-year-old about losing my job?"
#
# Kahiye determines:
#
# CHILD AGE
#
# WHAT CHILD ALREADY KNOWS
#
# EMOTIONAL IMPACT
#
# INFORMATION CHILD NEEDS
#
# INFORMATION CHILD DOES NOT NEED
#
# REASSURANCE THAT IS TRUE
#
# QUESTIONS CHILD MAY ASK
#
# FOLLOW-UP.
#
# ================================================================
# 241. HARD-CONVERSATION LIBRARY
# ================================================================
#
# Research age-appropriate conversations concerning:
#
# death
# divorce
# money problems
# unemployment
# illness
# disability
# puberty
# sex
# consent
# pornography
# drugs
# bullying
# racism/discrimination
# religion
# violence
# news
# war
# climate anxiety
# family conflict
# adoption
# mental health.
#
# ================================================================
# 242. DON'T LIE TO REASSURE
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should never advise:
#
# "Nothing bad will happen."
#
# when parent cannot know that.
#
# Teach:
#
# honest reassurance.
#
# Example:
#
# "I don't know exactly what will happen, but the adults are working on
# it and you will not have to handle it alone."
#
# ================================================================
# 243. NEWS + DISTRESSING EVENTS
# ================================================================
#
# Research how parents should discuss:
#
# war
# disasters
# terrorism
# violence
# political conflict
# disturbing news.
#
# Adapt to:
#
# age
# exposure
# proximity
# anxiety.
#
# ================================================================
# 244. DEATH / DISASTER FEED SAFETY
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye's content feed should not automatically surface distressing
# world events to children or parents unless context makes them useful.
#
# ================================================================
# 245. RELIGION / SPIRITUALITY
# ================================================================
#
# Families may have:
#
# religious
# spiritual
# secular
# mixed
#
# values.
#
# Kahiye should support family context without imposing belief.
#
# ================================================================
# 246. CASTE / CLASS / SOCIAL IDENTITY
# ================================================================
#
# India-specific research should consider age-appropriate conversations
# around:
#
# social inequality
# caste
# class
# discrimination
# privilege
# dignity
#
# where relevant.
#
# Guidance must be evidence-aware and culturally responsible.
#
# ================================================================
# 247. GENDER EXPECTATIONS
# ================================================================
#
# Research how gendered expectations may influence:
#
# emotion
# chores
# achievement
# independence
# appearance
# careers
# safety.
#
# Kahiye should not reinforce:
#
# "boys don't cry"
#
# "girls should..."
#
# stereotypes.
#
# ================================================================
# 248. EMOTIONAL VOCABULARY
# ================================================================
#
# Build age-progressive vocabulary:
#
# happy/sad/mad
#
# → disappointed/frustrated/worried
#
# → embarrassed/resentful/overwhelmed/ambivalent.
#
# ================================================================
# 249. EMOTION GRANULARITY
# ================================================================
#
# Research whether greater emotional differentiation supports regulation
# and communication.
#
# Avoid overstating evidence.
#
# ================================================================
# 250. EMOTION RECOGNITION
# ================================================================
#
# Exercises may use:
#
# stories
# situations
# facial/body cues
# personal reflection.
#
# Avoid pseudo-scientific:
#
# "read emotion perfectly from facial expression."
#
# Context matters.
#
# ================================================================
# 251. EMOTION GAME ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Generate age-appropriate:
#
# emotion charades
# story completion
# perspective questions
# drawing
# role play
# reflection cards.
#
# Every activity should contain:
#
# purpose
# age range
# duration
# parent instructions
# adaptation.
#
# ================================================================
# 252. GRATITUDE
# ================================================================
#
# Research gratitude interventions and limitations.
#
# Avoid compulsory:
#
# "Tell me three things you're grateful for"
#
# when child is distressed.
#
# Gratitude must not invalidate:
#
# sadness
# anger
# disappointment.
#
# ================================================================
# 253. MINDFULNESS
# ================================================================
#
# Research evidence for age-appropriate mindfulness.
#
# Avoid presenting mindfulness as:
#
# cure-all
# replacement for treatment.
#
# ================================================================
# 254. BREATHING
# ================================================================
#
# Breathing exercises can be offered as regulation tools.
#
# Do not imply:
#
# every distressed child will respond.
#
# Provide alternatives.
#
# ================================================================
# 255. REGULATION TOOLBOX
# ================================================================
#
# Build individualized options:
#
# movement
# breathing
# quiet space
# sensory input
# talking
# drawing
# music
# physical comfort
# problem solving
# time.
#
# Learn what actually works for this child.
#
# ================================================================
# 256. COPING ≠ SUPPRESSION
# ================================================================
#
# Regulation means:
#
# experiencing emotion safely and responding effectively.
#
# Not:
#
# eliminating unpleasant emotion.
#
# ================================================================
# 257. DISTRESS TOLERANCE
# ================================================================
#
# Research developmentally appropriate ways to build ability to:
#
# wait
# tolerate frustration
# cope with disappointment
# experience uncertainty.
#
# ================================================================
# 258. OVER-ACCOMMODATION
# ================================================================
#
# Research when parents may inadvertently maintain:
#
# anxiety
# avoidance
# dependency
#
# by removing every uncomfortable situation.
#
# Guidance must remain compassionate.
#
# ================================================================
# 259. CHALLENGE CALIBRATION
# ================================================================
#
# Aim for:
#
# SUPPORT
# +
# MANAGEABLE CHALLENGE.
#
# Not:
#
# rescue everything
#
# or:
#
# throw child into overwhelming distress.
#
# ================================================================
# 260. ZONE OF PROXIMAL SUPPORT
# ================================================================
#
# Research concepts related to:
#
# scaffolding
# guided participation
# fading support.
#
# Apply to:
#
# learning
# life skills
# emotional skills.
#
# ================================================================
# 261. PARENTING AS SCAFFOLDING
# ================================================================
#
# Long-term direction:
#
# MORE CHILD CAPABILITY
#
# → LESS PARENT CONTROL.
#
# ================================================================
# 262. DECISION TRANSFER
# ================================================================
#
# Build an age-progressive model for transferring decisions involving:
#
# clothes
# food
# homework
# friends
# devices
# activities
# money
# education
# career.
#
# ================================================================
# 263. RISK / AUTONOMY MATRIX
# ================================================================
#
# Higher risk:
#
# more adult oversight.
#
# Higher demonstrated capability:
#
# more autonomy.
#
# Avoid:
#
# age alone as sole variable.
#
# ================================================================
# 264. CAREER DEVELOPMENT
# ================================================================
#
# From later childhood/adolescence:
#
# interests
# strengths
# values
# exposure
# skills
# education options
# careers
# experimentation.
#
# ================================================================
# 265. CAREER ≠ DESTINY
# ================================================================
#
# Never tell a child:
#
# "Your profile indicates you should become an engineer."
#
# Instead:
#
# expose possibilities
# explore fit
# encourage experimentation.
#
# ================================================================
# 266. CAREER PRESSURE
# ================================================================
#
# Research family conflict around:
#
# medicine
# engineering
# business
# arts
# sports
# unconventional careers.
#
# Particularly examine Indian cultural/economic realities.
#
# ================================================================
# 267. FUTURE SKILLS
# ================================================================
#
# Research age-appropriate development of:
#
# communication
# creativity
# collaboration
# critical thinking
# digital literacy
# AI literacy
# adaptability
# financial literacy
# self-directed learning.
#
# Avoid speculative "future of work" hype.
#
# ================================================================
# 268. PRODUCT RECOMMENDATION ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye may recommend:
#
# books
# toys
# games
# educational materials
# safety products
# clothing
# developmental tools.
#
# Recommendation must begin:
#
# WHAT DEVELOPMENTAL NEED ARE WE SOLVING?
#
# Not:
#
# WHAT CAN WE SELL?
#
# ================================================================
# 269. COMMERCIAL INDEPENDENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Paid commercial relationships must NEVER alter:
#
# developmental advice
# safety advice
# product ranking
#
# without clear disclosure.
#
# ================================================================
# 270. PRODUCT RECOMMENDATION OBJECT
# ================================================================
#
# Store:
#
# need
# age/development
# product_category
# why_relevant
# evidence
# safety
# price_range
# alternatives
# sponsored_status.
#
# # ================================================================
# 271. AFFILIATE TRANSPARENCY
# ================================================================
#
# If Kahiye earns:
#
# affiliate commission
# referral fee
# sponsorship
# placement fee
#
# disclose it clearly.
#
# Never disguise:
#
# advertisement
#
# as:
#
# psychological/developmental recommendation.
#

# ================================================================
#
# Product recommendations involving:
#
# sleep
# feeding

# car seats
# medication

# baby equipment
# physical safety
#
# require heightened evidence and jurisdiction-aware safety checks.

# ================================================================
#
# Recommend books based on:
#
# developmental level
# reading ability
# interests
# emotional situation

# family context.
#
# Candidate categories:
#
# child reading
# parent-child reading
# parenting books
# activity books
# emotional-learning books.
#

# 274. MEDIA RECOMMENDATIONS
# ================================================================
#
# Research whether Kahiye should recommend:
#
# films
# television
# podcasts
# games
# educational apps.
#
# Consider:
#
# age
# content
# educational value
# violence
# sexual content
# advertising
# monetization
# privacy.

# ================================================================
# 275. COMMUNITY LAYER
# ================================================================
#
# Design the community as:
#
# PARENT-TO-PARENT SUPPORT
#
# moderated by:
#
# EVIDENCE
# SAFETY
# CIVILITY
# PRIVACY.
#
# Community experience should NOT become:
#
# competitive parenting
# medical misinformation
# shame
# fear
# unverified diagnosis
# commercial spam.

# 276. COMMUNITY QUESTION TYPES
# ================================================================
#
# Support:
#
# situation questions
# experience sharing
# practical tips
# emotional support
# local recommendations
# developmental discussion.
#
# ================================================================
# 277. EXPERIENCE ≠ ADVICE
# ================================================================
#
# Community UI should distinguish:
#
# "THIS WORKED FOR OUR FAMILY"
#
# from:
#
# "THIS IS MEDICALLY / DEVELOPMENTALLY RECOMMENDED."
#

# ================================================================
#
# Detect potentially dangerous claims involving:
#
# vaccines
# medication

# developmental disorders

# physical punishment
# supplements
# home remedies
# medical emergencies.
#
# ================================================================
# 279. COMMUNITY EVIDENCE LAYER
# ================================================================
#

#
# "WHAT THE EVIDENCE SAYS"
#
# separately from:
#
# parent experiences.

# 280. COMMUNITY MODERATION

# Research whether sensitive parenting questions should support:
#
# anonymous
#
# or:
#
# pseudonymous
#
# posting.
#
# ================================================================
# 283. COMMUNITY COMPARISON ANXIETY
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid feeds dominated by:
#
# "My 2-year-old reads."
#

#
# "My teenager got..."
#
# if they generate unhealthy developmental comparison.
#
# ================================================================
# 284. MILESTONE COMPETITION FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# Do not gamify:
#
# walking
# talking
# reading
# grades
# height
# weight
# developmental milestones.
#
# ================================================================
# 285. COMMUNITY AI SUMMARIZER
# ================================================================
#
# AI may summarize:
#
# recurring parent experiences
# useful suggestions
# disagreements
# evidence.
#
# It must explicitly separate:
#
# COMMUNITY EXPERIENCE
#
# from:
#
# VERIFIED GUIDANCE.
#
# ================================================================
# 286. LONGITUDINAL CHILD MEMORY SYSTEM
# ================================================================
#
# This is one of Kahiye's most important capabilities.
#
# Design an 18-year developmental memory architecture.
#
# The system must remember useful context without creating:
#
# permanent childhood labels
# surveillance dossiers
# false psychological certainty.
#
# ================================================================
# 287. MEMORY TYPES
# ================================================================
#
# Research and design separate memory classes:
#
# CHILD FACTS
#
# PARENT FACTS
#
# FAMILY FACTS
#
# DEVELOPMENTAL OBSERVATIONS
#
# MILESTONES
#
# HEALTH CONTEXT
#
# EDUCATION CONTEXT
#
# BEHAVIOR EVENTS
#
# EMOTIONAL EVENTS
#
# FAMILY EVENTS
#
# INTERVENTIONS TRIED
#
# OUTCOMES
#
# PREFERENCES
#
# STRENGTHS
#
# INTERESTS
#
# ROUTINES
#
# PROFESSIONAL GUIDANCE
#
# PARENT REFLECTIONS.
#
# ================================================================
# 288. FACT ≠ OBSERVATION ≠ INFERENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Every memory should distinguish:
#
# FACT
#
# "School starts at 8:30."
#
# OBSERVATION
#
# "Parent reports three difficult drop-offs this week."
#
# INFERENCE
#
# "Separation may be contributing."
#
# HYPOTHESIS
#
# "If difficulty is strongest after poor sleep, fatigue may be a
# contributing factor."
#
# Never store inference as fact.
#
# ================================================================
# 289. MEMORY PROVENANCE
# ================================================================
#
# Every important memory should record:
#
# who provided it
# when
# context
# source
# confidence
# whether parent confirmed it
# whether professional supplied it.
#
# ================================================================
# 290. MEMORY CONFIDENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Use:
#
# VERIFIED
#
# PARENT-REPORTED
#
# CHILD-REPORTED where appropriate
#
# AI-INFERRED
#
# PROFESSIONAL-REPORTED
#
# SYSTEM-OBSERVED.
#
# ================================================================
# 291. MEMORY RECENCY
# ================================================================
#
# Children change rapidly.
#
# A statement from age 4 should not automatically describe the child
# at age 14.
#
# Every behavioral/trait memory requires:
#
# timestamp
# developmental context
# recency weighting.
#
# ================================================================
# 292. MEMORY DECAY
# ================================================================
#
# Research how stale behavioral inferences should lose influence over
# future recommendations.
#
# Facts may persist.
#
# Interpretations should decay.
#
# ================================================================
# 293. MEMORY CONTRADICTION
# ================================================================
#
# If old memory says:
#
# "difficulty separating"
#
# and newer observations show:
#
# independent school entry,
#
# update the model.
#
# Do not preserve obsolete identity labels.
#
# ================================================================
# 294. MEMORY VERSIONING
# ================================================================
#
# Never silently overwrite important developmental history.
#
# Store:
#
# previous state
# updated state
# reason
# date.
#
# ================================================================
# 295. EVENT MEMORY
# ================================================================
#
# Important events may include:
#
# school change
# sibling birth
# relocation
# divorce
# bereavement
# illness
# hospitalization
# bullying
# exam period
# major achievement
# family stress.
#
# These may explain temporary behavioral changes.
#
# ================================================================
# 296. INTERVENTION MEMORY
# ================================================================
#
# Store:
#
# problem
# suggested strategy
# parent attempted?
# context
# outcome
# parent assessment
# child response.
#
# ================================================================
# 297. LEARN WHAT WORKS FOR THIS CHILD
# ================================================================
#
# Over time Kahiye should learn:
#
# "Advance warnings appear to help transitions."
#
# rather than repeatedly recommending:
#
# generic parenting advice.
#
# ================================================================
# 298. DO NOT OVERLEARN
# ================================================================
#
# One successful intervention does not establish:
#
# permanent rule.
#
# Track repeated outcomes.
#
# ================================================================
# 299. LONGITUDINAL PATTERN DETECTION
# ================================================================
#
# Potential patterns:
#
# behavior after poor sleep
# school-night anxiety
# sibling conflict triggers
# exam stress
# transition difficulty
# successful calming strategies
# emerging interests
# confidence changes.
#
# ================================================================
# 300. CORRELATION WARNING
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye may detect:
#
# "On five recorded difficult mornings, sleep was shorter."
#
# It should NOT automatically conclude:
#
# "Sleep deprivation causes this child's behavior."
#
# ================================================================
# 301. DEVELOPMENTAL TIMELINE
# ================================================================
#
# Create a human-readable timeline containing:
#
# milestones
# transitions
# interests
# family events
# challenges
# successful strategies
# achievements
# meaningful memories.
#
# ================================================================
# 302. MEMORY SHOULD ALSO HOLD JOY
# ================================================================
#
# Do not let longitudinal memory become:
#
# problem database.
#
# Store:
#
# funny moments
# interests
# strengths
# achievements
# family rituals
# meaningful experiences
# growth.
#
# ================================================================
# 303. CHILD'S OWN VOICE
# ================================================================
#
# As child matures, research whether Kahiye should allow:
#
# child reflections
# preferences
# goals
# self-described interests
#
# under appropriate privacy/consent architecture.
#
# ================================================================
# 304. CHILD DATA ETHICS
# ================================================================
#
# This is a foundational research area.
#
# Kahiye may potentially hold:
#
# 18 YEARS OF HIGHLY SENSITIVE INFORMATION
#
# about a person who initially cannot consent.
#
# Treat this as a first-class ethical/product problem.
#
# ================================================================
# 305. CHILD DATA CONSTITUTION
# ================================================================
#
# Research and propose explicit principles:
#
# DATA MINIMIZATION
#
# PURPOSE LIMITATION
#
# CHILD DIGNITY
#
# SECURITY
#
# TRANSPARENCY
#
# PARENT CONTROL
#
# AGE-APPROPRIATE CHILD CONTROL
#
# DELETION
#
# PORTABILITY
#
# PROVENANCE
#
# NO EXPLOITATIVE PROFILING.
#
# ================================================================
# 306. DON'T STORE EVERYTHING BECAUSE YOU CAN
# ================================================================
#
# Ask for every memory field:
#
# WILL THIS IMPROVE FUTURE PARENTING GUIDANCE ENOUGH TO JUSTIFY
# RETAINING IT?
#
# If not:
#
# don't persist it.
#
# ================================================================
# 307. SENSITIVE DATA CLASSES
# ================================================================
#
# Identify heightened protections for:
#
# health
# mental health
# sexuality
# abuse
# disability
# school
# location
# biometrics
# photographs
# family conflict
# religion
# identity.
#
# ================================================================
# 308. PRIVACY RESEARCH
# ================================================================
#
# Research current applicable requirements including:
#
# INDIA DPDP FRAMEWORK
#
# child-specific Indian requirements
#
# COPPA where relevant
#
# GDPR / GDPR-K where relevant
#
# UK children's privacy requirements where relevant
#
# platform/app-store requirements.
#
# Verify CURRENT law/regulation at execution time.
#
# ================================================================
# 309. INDIA CHILD DATA
# ================================================================
#
# Perform a dedicated legal/product research workstream on:
#
# Digital Personal Data Protection Act / Rules as currently applicable
# verifiable parental consent
# definition of child
# tracking/behavioral monitoring restrictions
# targeted advertising implications
# withdrawal
# deletion
# breach response.
#
# Do not rely on outdated summaries.
#
# ================================================================
# 310. PARENT CONSENT IS NOT UNLIMITED
# ================================================================
#
# Research the ethical distinction between:
#
# parent authorization
#
# and:
#
# child's evolving privacy interest.
#
# ================================================================
# 311. EVOLVING AUTONOMY
# ================================================================
#
# Design privacy transitions:
#
# INFANCY:
# parent controls.
#
# CHILDHOOD:
# age-appropriate explanation begins.
#
# PRE-TEEN:
# greater transparency.
#
# TEEN:
# meaningful privacy/autonomy controls where legally/product-wise
# appropriate.
#
# AGE 18:
# explicit ownership/transfer/deletion decision.
#
# ================================================================
# 312. AGE-18 HANDOVER
# ================================================================
#
# This is critical.
#
# Research what should happen when child becomes an adult.
#
# Possible options:
#
# transfer selected developmental record
# allow deletion
# allow export
# preserve only parent-owned journal material
# separate parent memory from child's personal record.
#
# Design explicitly.
#
# ================================================================
# 313. PARENT JOURNAL VS CHILD RECORD
# ================================================================
#
# These should not necessarily be the same database concept.
#
# Example:
#
# "I felt overwhelmed today."
#
# belongs to:
#
# PARENT JOURNAL.
#
# "Child had fever."
#
# may belong to:
#
# CHILD RECORD.
#
# Separate ownership/access rules.
#
# ================================================================
# 314. MULTI-PARENT ACCESS
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# parent permissions
# separated parents
# guardians
# grandparents
# caregivers
# legal custody
# restricted access.
#
# ================================================================
# 315. FAMILY ACCESS CONTROL
# ================================================================
#
# Build role-based access:
#
# PRIMARY GUARDIAN
#
# CO-GUARDIAN
#
# CAREGIVER
#
# TEEN
#
# PROFESSIONAL where explicitly authorized.
#
# ================================================================
# 316. FAMILY CONFLICT + DATA
# ================================================================
#
# Consider hostile contexts:
#
# divorce
# custody disputes
# domestic abuse
# coercive control.
#
# Child/family data must not become:
#
# surveillance weapon.
#
# ================================================================
# 317. LOCATION DATA
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid collecting precise location unless genuinely required.
#
# If used:
#
# explain purpose
