# propaganda
# misinformation
# satire
# AI-generated content.
#
# ================================================================
# 552. ADVERTISING LITERACY
# ================================================================
#
# Children should progressively understand:
#
# ads are designed to influence.
#
# Include:
#
# influencer marketing
# product placement
# scarcity
# social proof
# targeted advertising.
#
# ================================================================
# 553. PERSUASION + CHILDREN
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye must NOT use:
#
# manipulative psychological persuasion
#
# to force children into compliance.
#
# ================================================================
# 554. NLP ETHICAL FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# Any retained NLP-inspired communication technique must satisfy:
#
# TRANSPARENCY
#
# RESPECT FOR AUTONOMY
#
# NO COVERT MANIPULATION
#
# NO FALSE SCIENTIFIC CLAIM
#
# NO EXPLOITATION OF CHILD VULNERABILITY.
#
# ================================================================
# 555. COMMUNICATION GOAL
# ================================================================
#
# Goal:
#
# understanding
# cooperation
# regulation
# skill
# relationship.
#
# Not:
#
# psychological control.
#
# ================================================================
# 556. PERSUASION VS COERCION
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# influence
# negotiation
# incentives
# boundaries
# coercion.
#
# Teach parents the difference.
#
# ================================================================
# 557. CHOICE ARCHITECTURE
# ================================================================
#
# Structured choices may help:
#
# autonomy
# cooperation.
#
# Example:
#
# "Blue pajamas or green?"
#
# But do not offer fake choices:
#
# "Do you want to go to school?"
#
# when attendance is not optional.
#
# ================================================================
# 558. FALSE CHOICE FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# Parent communication should remain:
#
# honest.
#
# ================================================================
# 559. NATURAL CONSEQUENCES SAFETY
# ================================================================
#
# Never allow dangerous:
#
# "natural consequences."
#
# Example:
#
# child refusing seatbelt
#
# does NOT mean:
#
# allow natural consequence.
#
# Safety overrides learning-through-consequence.
#
# ================================================================
# 560. DEVELOPMENTAL ETHICS
# ================================================================
#
# A child is not:
#
# a behavioral optimization project.
#
# Kahiye must preserve:
#
# agency
# dignity
# individuality
# play
# relationship
# childhood.
#
# ================================================================
# 561. OPTIMIZATION FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# Do not optimize every:
#
# hour
# habit
# activity
# conversation
#
# for:
#
# future success.
#
# ================================================================
# 562. CHILDHOOD HAS INTRINSIC VALUE
# ================================================================
#
# Research/product philosophy must recognize:
#
# joy
# play
# rest
# curiosity
# relationships
#
# as:
#
# valuable in themselves.
#
# ================================================================
# 563. SUCCESS DEFINITION
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye must not define successful child as:
#
# wealthy
# academically elite
# highly productive
# socially dominant.
#
# ================================================================
# 564. BROAD DEVELOPMENTAL OUTCOMES
# ================================================================
#
# Candidate outcomes:
#
# wellbeing
# secure relationships
# emotional competence
# agency
# resilience
# responsibility
# empathy
# curiosity
# capability
# values
# adaptability.
#
# Research rather than treating this list as universal doctrine.
#
# ================================================================
# 565. PARENT GOALS VS CHILD AUTONOMY
# ================================================================
#
# Parent may want:
#
# IIT
# medicine
# family business
# elite sport.
#
# Child may not.
#
# Kahiye should help:
#
# distinguish parent aspiration
#
# from:
#
# child's developing goals.
#
# ================================================================
# 566. CHILD GOAL SYSTEM
# ================================================================
#
# As children mature, allow goals to become:
#
# CHILD-OWNED
#
# rather than:
#
# PARENT-ASSIGNED.
#
# ================================================================
# 567. TEEN GOAL COACHING
# ================================================================
#
# Support:
#
# goal definition
# planning
# obstacles
# review
# autonomy.
#
# Do not create:
#
# surveillance productivity system.
#
# ================================================================
# 568. TEEN TRUST
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# trust building
# broken trust
# rebuilding trust
# monitoring
# independence.
#
# ================================================================
# 569. TRUST IS BIDIRECTIONAL
# ================================================================
#
# Parents expect:
#
# honesty.
#
# Teenagers also need:
#
# predictable
# honest
# proportionate
#
# parenting.
#
# ================================================================
# 570. BROKEN RULES
# ================================================================
#
# When teenager breaks rule:
#
# determine:
#
# safety impact
# intentionality
# pattern
# reason
# trust impact
# appropriate consequence
# repair.
#
# ================================================================
# 571. CURFEW
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# age
# context
# transport
# neighborhood
# safety
# demonstrated responsibility.
#
# No universal curfew.
#
# ================================================================
# 572. TEEN PRIVACY
# ================================================================
#
# Parent asks:
#
# "Should I read her messages?"
#
# Provide decision framework:
#
# safety concern
# evidence
# proportionality
# age
# trust
# less-invasive options.
#
# ================================================================
# 573. TEEN CONFIDENTIALITY
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# adolescent healthcare confidentiality
# counseling confidentiality
#
# by jurisdiction where relevant.
#
# Do not invent legal rules.
#
# ================================================================
# 574. DATING RULES
# ================================================================
#
# Help families develop:
#
# safety
# values
# consent
# communication
# realistic boundaries.
#
# Avoid:
#
# gender double standards.
#
# ================================================================
# 575. HEARTBREAK
# ================================================================
#
# Do not minimize:
#
# "It's just teenage love."
#
# Support:
#
# grief
# perspective
# connection
# functioning.
#
# ================================================================
# 576. PEER REJECTION
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# belonging
# exclusion
# loneliness
# social skills
# school context.
#
# ================================================================
# 577. LONELINESS
# ================================================================
#
# Distinguish:
#
# solitude
#
# from:
#
# unwanted isolation.
#
# ================================================================
# 578. INTROVERSION
# ================================================================
#
# Do not treat:
#
# introversion
#
# as:
#
# social deficit.
#
# ================================================================
# 579. SHYNESS
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid permanently labeling:
#
# "She's shy."
#
# especially in front of child.
#
# Describe:
#
# "She takes time to warm up in new groups."
#
# ================================================================
# 580. SOCIAL SKILLS
# ================================================================
#
# Teach:
#
# greeting
# joining play
# conversation
# listening
# disagreement
# boundaries
# exit.
#
# ================================================================
# 581. SOCIAL COACHING WITHOUT SCRIPTING CHILD
# ================================================================
#
# Parent can rehearse:
#
# possibilities.
#
# Child should not become:
#
# actor performing parent's social script.
#
# ================================================================
# 582. POPULARITY
# ================================================================
#
# Do not optimize:
#
# popularity.
#
# Focus:
#
# belonging
# reciprocal friendship
# safety
# social competence.
#
# ================================================================
# 583. STATUS
# ================================================================
#
# Research adolescent status dynamics:
#
# peer hierarchy
# social media
# brands
# appearance
# popularity.
#
# ================================================================
# 584. CONSUMERISM
# ================================================================
#
# Parent asks:
#
# "Everyone has an iPhone except my child."
#
# Explore:
#
# belonging
# affordability
# values
# functionality
# peer pressure.
#
# ================================================================
# 585. ENTITLEMENT
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid moral labels.
#
# Examine:
#
# expectations
# reinforcement
# gratitude
# contribution
# boundaries
# socioeconomic context.
#
# ================================================================
# 586. GRATITUDE WITHOUT GUILT
# ================================================================
#
# Do not teach gratitude through:
#
# "Other children have nothing."
#
# Research healthier:
#
# appreciation
# perspective
# generosity.
#
# # ================================================================
# 587. EMPATHY WITHOUT PARENTIFICATION
# ================================================================
#
# Children should learn:
#
# empathy.
#
# They should not become responsible for:
#
# regulating parent's emotions
# resolving adult conflict
# supporting parent psychologically
# protecting parent from loneliness
# mediating between parents.
#
# Research:
#
# parentification
# emotional role reversal
# age-appropriate family contribution.
#
# ================================================================
# 588. PARENT EMOTIONAL DISCLOSURE
# ================================================================
#
# Parents can appropriately show:
#
# sadness
# disappointment
# frustration
# vulnerability.
#
# But disclosure should remain:
#
# age appropriate
# bounded
# emotionally safe.
#
# Kahiye should help distinguish:
#
# "I'm sad today. Adults feel sad sometimes, and I'm taking care of
# myself."
#
# from:
#
# making child responsible for:
#
# "You're the only person who understands me."
#
# ================================================================
# 589. CHILD AS CONFIDANT FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should detect when parent may be using child as:
#
# therapist
# relationship counselor
# messenger
# emotional partner
# ally against another parent.
#
# Respond without shaming.
#
# Redirect adult emotional needs toward:
#
# appropriate adult support.
#
# ================================================================
# 590. TRIANGULATION
# ================================================================
#
# Research family-system patterns where child becomes:
#
# mediator
# messenger
# judge
# ally
#
# between adults.
#
# Example:
#
# "Tell your father he needs to..."
#
# Kahiye should recommend:
#
# adult-to-adult communication
#
# wherever safe and appropriate.
#
# ================================================================
# 591. ADULT CONFLICT EXPOSURE
# ================================================================
#
# Research effects of:
#
# frequent
# intense
# unresolved
#
# interparental conflict.
#
# Distinguish:
#
# ordinary disagreement
#
# from:
#
# frightening/destructive conflict.
#
# ================================================================
# 592. CONFLICT REPAIR MODELING
# ================================================================
#
# Children may benefit from seeing adults:
#
# disagree respectfully
# listen
# apologize
# compromise
# repair.
#
# Do not imply:
#
# children must never witness disagreement.
#
# ================================================================
# 593. DOMESTIC VIOLENCE
# ================================================================
#
# If conversation suggests:
#
# coercive control
# domestic violence
# threats
# physical danger
#
# ordinary:
#
# "communicate better with your partner"
#
# advice may be unsafe.
#
# Route to:
#
# safety-specialized guidance.
#
# ================================================================
# 594. COERCIVE CONTROL
# ================================================================
#
# Research indicators such as:
#
# monitoring
# isolation
# financial control
# threats
# intimidation
# technology surveillance.
#
# Kahiye must not instruct a victim to confront an abusive partner if
# doing so could increase risk.
#
# ================================================================
# 595. FAMILY SAFETY PLAN
# ================================================================
#
# For appropriate high-risk contexts:
#
# use professionally validated safety-planning principles.
#
# Localize:
#
# emergency resources
# trusted contacts
# legal/support pathways.
#
# ================================================================
# 596. PARENT ANGER
# ================================================================
#
# Parent may say:
#
# "I'm so angry I'm scared I'll hit him."
#
# Kahiye should immediately prioritize:
#
# CHILD SAFETY
#
# PARENT DE-ESCALATION
#
# DISTANCE IF SAFE
#
# ANOTHER RESPONSIBLE ADULT
#
# PROFESSIONAL/EMERGENCY SUPPORT IF NEEDED.
#
# Do not respond first with:
#
# developmental theory.
#
# ================================================================
# 597. PARENT LOSS OF CONTROL
# ================================================================
#
# Build:
#
# STOP PLAN.
#
# Research appropriate immediate steps such as:
#
# make child safe
# pause interaction
# reduce stimulation
# ask another safe caregiver to take over
# regulate
# return later.
#
# ================================================================
# 598. AFTER PARENT SHOUTS
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should provide:
#
# regulation
# accountability
# repair
# prevention.
#
# Avoid:
#
# "Don't worry, every parent shouts."
#
# if that minimizes repeated harmful behavior.
#
# ================================================================
# 599. PATTERN FREQUENCY MATTERS
# ================================================================
#
# One regretted incident
#
# differs from:
#
# frequent intimidation.
#
# Track:
#
# frequency
# intensity
# impact
# escalation.
#
# ================================================================
# 600. PARENT SAFETY ESCALATION
# ================================================================
#
# If parent repeatedly fears harming child:
#
# encourage:
#
# immediate support
# professional intervention
# safety planning.
#
# ================================================================
# 601. PARENT GUILT
# ================================================================
#
# Distinguish:
#
# USEFUL GUILT
#
# "I acted against my values."
#
# from:
#
# GLOBAL SHAME
#
# "I am a terrible parent."
#
# Help convert:
#
# guilt
#
# into:
#
# repair + change.
#
# ================================================================
# 602. PARENT SHAME
# ================================================================
#
# Shame may reduce:
#
# openness
# help-seeking
# reflective capacity.
#
# Kahiye should correct behavior without defining parent identity.
#
# ================================================================
# 603. PERFECTIONISTIC PARENTING
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# intensive parenting
# parental perfectionism
# social comparison
# parenting influencers
# optimization culture.
#
# ================================================================
# 604. PARENTING SOCIAL COMPARISON
# ================================================================
#
# Parent may compare:
#
# milestones
# schools
# grades
# behavior
# activities
# travel
# possessions
# parenting styles.
#
# Kahiye should help distinguish:
#
# useful benchmark
#
# from:
#
# anxiety-producing comparison.
#
# ================================================================
# 605. PARENT SOCIAL MEDIA
# ================================================================
#
# Research effects of:
#
# idealized parenting content
# parenting advice overload
# contradictory advice
# influencer authority
# parent guilt.
#
# ================================================================
# 606. INFORMATION OVERLOAD
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should reduce:
#
# parenting-information burden.
#
# It should not become:
#
# another source of 50 things parents should be doing.
#
# ================================================================
# 607. ONE THING AT A TIME
# ================================================================
#
# When several improvements are possible:
#
# prioritize:
#
# highest-leverage
# safest
# easiest
# most relevant
#
# intervention.
#
# ================================================================
# 608. PARENT ATTENTION BUDGET
# ================================================================
#
# Treat parental attention as:
#
# finite.
#
# Advice should respect:
#
# work
# sleep
# household
# siblings
# relationships
# health.
#
# ================================================================
# 609. FAMILY LOAD MODEL
# ================================================================
#
# Research a nonjudgmental way to understand:
#
# caregiving load
# work load
# financial stress
# health burden
# household responsibilities
# support availability.
#
# ================================================================
# 610. RECOMMENDATION FEASIBILITY
# ================================================================
#
# Before recommending:
#
# daily one-on-one activities
# elaborate meal routines
# complex exercises
#
# consider:
#
# family capacity.
#
# ================================================================
# 611. LOW-BANDWIDTH PARENTING MODE
# ================================================================
#
# Parent may say:
#
# "I have no energy. Just tell me the most important thing."
#
# Kahiye should respond accordingly.
#
# ================================================================
# 612. CRISIS ≠ CURRICULUM MOMENT
# ================================================================
#
# During:
#
# acute family crisis
# bereavement
# illness
# financial emergency
#
# reduce:
#
# optimization expectations.
#
# ================================================================
# 613. FAMILY FINANCIAL STRESS
# ================================================================
#
# Research how:
#
# financial stress
# unemployment
# housing instability
#
# can affect:
#
# parent stress
# child wellbeing
# family conflict.
#
# ================================================================
# 614. TALKING TO CHILDREN ABOUT MONEY PROBLEMS
# ================================================================
#
# Help parents communicate:
#
# honestly
# age-appropriately
# without burdening child with adult responsibility.
#
# ================================================================
# 615. POVERTY / RESOURCE CONSTRAINTS
# ================================================================
#
# Never recommend:
#
# expensive products
# private therapy
# elite extracurriculars
#
# as though universally accessible.
#
# Provide:
#
# low-cost
# free
# public
# community
#
# alternatives where possible.
#
# ================================================================
# 616. ACCESS-TO-CARE INTELLIGENCE
# ================================================================
#
# When professional help is advised:
#
# research pathways for:
#
# private care
# public hospitals
# schools
# NGOs
# telehealth
# community services.
#
# ================================================================
# 617. DISABILITY-INCLUSIVE PARENTING
# ================================================================
#
# Research parenting support for children with:
#
# physical disabilities
# intellectual disabilities
# sensory disabilities
# developmental disabilities
# chronic conditions.
#
# ================================================================
# 618. ACCESSIBILITY ≠ NORMALIZATION PRESSURE
# ================================================================
#
# Goal should not always be:
#
# make child behave indistinguishably from peers.
#
# Consider:
#
# participation
# communication
# autonomy
# comfort
# dignity
# accommodation.
#
# ================================================================
# 619. CHRONIC ILLNESS
# ================================================================
#
# Research family challenges involving:
#
# medication
# hospital visits
# school absence
# pain
# uncertainty
# sibling impact
# parent stress.
#
# Medical management remains with qualified professionals.
#
# ================================================================
# 620. HOSPITAL PREPARATION
# ================================================================
#
# Generate age-appropriate preparation for:
#
# procedure
# surgery
# blood test
# hospitalization
# doctor visit.
#
# Do not falsely promise:
#
# "It won't hurt."
#
# ================================================================
# 621. MEDICAL HONESTY
# ================================================================
#
# Teach truthful preparation:
#
# what child may see
# hear
# feel
# what parent knows
# what parent does not know.
#
# ================================================================
# 622. MEDICAL ANXIETY
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# preparation
# choice where possible
# distraction
# coping
# comfort
# exposure principles.
#
# ================================================================
# 623. PAIN COMMUNICATION
# ================================================================
#
# Support parent in helping child describe:
#
# location
# intensity
# quality
# timing
#
# without attempting diagnosis.
#
# ================================================================
# 624. NEURODIVERGENT COMMUNICATION
# ================================================================
#
# Adapt guidance where child has known:
#
# autism
# ADHD
# language differences
# intellectual disability
# sensory needs.
#
# ================================================================
# 625. AUTISM-INFORMED SUPPORT
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# communication
# sensory environment
# predictability
# transitions
# interests
# regulation
# autonomy.
#
# Avoid:
#
# cure narratives
# deficit-only framing
# unsupported treatments.
#
# ================================================================
# 626. ADHD-INFORMED SUPPORT
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# executive function
# environment
# routines
# reinforcement
# school support
# emotional regulation.
#
# Avoid:
#
# "Try harder"
# "needs more discipline."
#
# ================================================================
# 627. DYSLEXIA / LEARNING DIFFERENCES
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# evidence-based literacy support
# accommodations
# confidence
# school communication.
#
# Do not recommend:
#
# unsupported miracle programs.
#
# ================================================================
# 628. SENSORY DIFFERENCES
# ================================================================
#
# Parent may report:
#
# clothing intolerance
# noise sensitivity
# food texture
# crowded spaces.
#
# Generate:
#
# possible contextual interpretations
# accommodations
# observation
# professional escalation where warranted.
#
# ================================================================
# 629. SENSORY LABEL CAUTION
# ================================================================
#
# Do not diagnose:
#
# "sensory processing disorder"
#
# solely from app conversation.
#
# ================================================================
# 630. DEVELOPMENTAL SERVICES DIRECTORY
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# pediatric developmental services
# speech/language
# occupational therapy
# special education
# psychological assessment.
#
# ================================================================
# 631. EVIDENCE-BASED INTERVENTION DIRECTORY
# ================================================================
#
# Build knowledge of:
#
# interventions
# intended population
# evidence strength
# age range
# provider qualifications
# limitations.
#
# ================================================================
# 632. UNPROVEN THERAPY FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# Flag:
#
# miracle cures
# detoxes
# unsupported supplements
# pseudoscientific developmental therapies
#
# where evidence is weak/absent.
#
# ================================================================
# 633. PARENT DESPERATION VULNERABILITY
# ================================================================
#
# Parents facing developmental concerns may be especially vulnerable
# to:
#
# expensive unsupported treatments.
#
# Kahiye should protect rather than exploit this vulnerability.
#
# ================================================================
# 634. TREATMENT CLAIM EVIDENCE
# ================================================================
#
# For intervention claims distinguish:
#
# improves symptom
# improves functioning
# parent-reported benefit
# mechanistic hypothesis
# no established benefit.
#
# ================================================================
# 635. MEDICAL / PSYCHOLOGICAL DISCLAIMER UX
# ================================================================
#
# Do not plaster every answer with:
#
# generic disclaimers.
#
# Surface boundaries:
#
# when relevant.
#
# ================================================================
# 636. SAFETY MICROCOPY
# ================================================================
#
# Safety language should be:
#
# clear
# calm
# specific.
#
# Avoid:
#
# legalistic boilerplate.
#
# ================================================================
# 637. AGENT UNCERTAINTY
# ================================================================
#
# Agents must be able to state:
#
# "I can't tell from this alone."
#
# "There are several plausible explanations."
#
# "This deserves professional assessment."
#
# ================================================================
# 638. EXPLANATION OF UNCERTAINTY
# ================================================================
#
# When useful explain:
#
# WHAT INFORMATION WOULD REDUCE UNCERTAINTY?
#
# ================================================================
# 639. CHILD OBSERVATION MODE
# ================================================================
#
# Allow parent to record:
#
# what happened
# context
# child response
# parent response.
#
# Avoid requiring:
#
# psychological interpretation.
#
# ================================================================
# 640. STRUCTURED OBSERVATION
# ================================================================
#
# Candidate fields:
#
# date/time
# context
# people present
# antecedent
# behavior
# duration
# intensity
# response
# outcome
# sleep/hunger where relevant.
#
# Make logging:
#
# optional
# lightweight.
#
# ================================================================
# 641. VOICE LOGGING
# ================================================================
#
# Parent should be able to say:
#
# "Log this: she had another meltdown after swimming..."
#
# AI extracts structure.
#
# Parent confirms important interpretation.
#
# ================================================================
# 642. PHOTO / VIDEO INPUT
# ================================================================
#
# Research whether future Kahiye should analyze:
#
# drawings
# school work
# photos
# videos.
#
# This requires:
#
# heightened privacy
# consent
# safety
# validity analysis.
#
# ================================================================
# 643. NO EMOTION READING FROM FACE AS CERTAINTY
# ================================================================
#
# Do not claim:
#
# child's internal emotional state
#
# from:
#
# facial image alone.
#
# ================================================================
# 644. NO DEVELOPMENTAL DIAGNOSIS FROM VIDEO
# ================================================================
#
# Video may support:
#
# parent observation
#
# but not:
#
# autonomous diagnosis.
#
# ================================================================
# 645. DOCUMENT INPUT
# ================================================================
#
# Potential uploads:
#
# school report
# pediatric note
# assessment
# teacher communication.
#
# AI should:
#
# explain
# summarize
# help formulate questions.
#
# ================================================================
# 646. PROFESSIONAL DOCUMENT PRIORITY
# ================================================================
#
# Do not overwrite:
#
# professional findings
#
# with:
#
# casual AI inference.
#
# ================================================================
# 647. DOCUMENT PROVENANCE
# ================================================================
#
# Record:
#
# document source
# date
# author/professional
# relevant child
# extraction confidence.
#
# ================================================================
# 648. MULTIMODAL PRIVACY
# ================================================================
#
# Images/audio/video of children are highly sensitive.
#
# Research:
#
# local processing
# encryption
# retention
# deletion
# model-training exclusion
# access control.
#
# ================================================================
# 649. NO DEFAULT TRAINING ON CHILD DATA
# ================================================================
#
# Research/design strong default:
#
# private child/family data is NOT used to train generalized models
# without a legally and ethically appropriate explicit framework.
#
# ================================================================
# 650. SECURITY ARCHITECTURE
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# encryption at rest
# encryption in transit
# key management
# access control
# audit logs
# breach detection
# secure backups
# data segregation.
#
# ================================================================
# 651. CHILD PROFILE IS HIGH-VALUE SENSITIVE DATA
# ================================================================
#
# Threat model should assume:
#
# attackers may value:
#
# identity
# school
# family
# health
# location
# photographs.
#
# ================================================================
# 652. SECURITY THREAT MODEL
# ================================================================
#
# Test:
#
# account takeover
# credential stuffing
# malicious caregiver
# separated parent
# insider access
# database breach
# API leak
# prompt injection
# malicious upload
# third-party SDK leakage.
#
# ================================================================
# 653. THIRD-PARTY SDK AUDIT
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid sending child data unnecessarily to:
#
# analytics
# advertising
# crash reporting
# third-party AI
# attribution SDKs.
#
# ================================================================
# 654. AI PROVIDER DATA MAP
# ================================================================
#
# For every model/API:
#
# what data leaves Kahiye?
#
# where?
#
# retention?
#
# training?
#
# jurisdiction?
#
# contract?
#
# # ================================================================
# 655. SENSITIVE-DATA ROUTER
# ================================================================
#
# Before sending any user content to:
#
# external LLM
# embedding service
# analytics provider
# recommendation engine
# search engine
#
# classify:
#
# DOES THIS CONTAIN SENSITIVE CHILD/FAMILY DATA?
#
# If yes:
#
# determine whether external processing is:
#
# NECESSARY
# PERMITTED
# MINIMIZED
# DISCLOSED
# SECURE.
#
# ================================================================
# 656. DATA REDACTION
# ================================================================
#
# Where possible, remove unnecessary:
#
# names
# addresses
# schools
# phone numbers
# precise locations
# identifying medical information
#
# before external model calls.
#
# Example:
#
# Instead of sending:
#
# "Aarav Sharma, age 9, studying at XYZ School in Mumbai..."
#
# external reasoning may only require:
#
# "9-year-old child."
#
# ================================================================
# 657. DATA MINIMIZATION BY TASK
# ================================================================
#
# Every agent receives:
#
# ONLY THE CONTEXT IT NEEDS.
#
# Example:
#
# product recommendation agent
#
# does not require:
#
# parental marital conflict history.
#
# ================================================================
# 658. AGENT PERMISSION SCOPES
# ================================================================
#
# Define data-access scopes for:
#
# DEVELOPMENT AGENT
# BEHAVIOR AGENT
# COMMUNICATION AGENT
# HEALTH AGENT
# SAFETY AGENT
# CONTENT AGENT
# PRODUCT AGENT
# COMMUNITY AGENT.
#
# Apply:
#
# LEAST PRIVILEGE.
#
# ================================================================
# 659. MEMORY ACCESS CONTROL
# ================================================================
#
# Memory should not be:
#
# one giant context blob
#
# automatically provided to every agent.
#
# Retrieve:
#
# relevant
# permissioned
# minimally sufficient
#
# context.
#
# ================================================================
# 660. SENSITIVE MEMORY CLASSES
# ================================================================
#
# Consider additional protection for:
#
# abuse history
# sexuality
# mental health
# medical diagnosis
# reproductive health
# adoption
# custody
# domestic violence
# precise location.
#
# ================================================================
# 661. DATA RETENTION POLICY
# ================================================================
#
# Define retention by:
#
# DATA TYPE
#
# PURPOSE
#
# AGE
#
# SENSITIVITY
#
# USER EXPECTATION
#
# LEGAL REQUIREMENT.
#
# Do not default everything to:
#
# retain forever.
#
# ================================================================
# 662. TEMPORARY CONTEXT
# ================================================================
#
# Some information should exist only for:
#
# current conversation
#
# and then:
#
# expire.
#
# ================================================================
# 663. MEMORY SUGGESTION
# ================================================================
#
# For potentially useful but sensitive information:
#
# consider asking:
#
# "Would you like Kahiye to remember this for future guidance?"
#
# ================================================================
# 664. CHILD DATA EXPLANATION
# ================================================================
#
# Parents should be able to understand:
#
# WHAT KAHIYE KNOWS
#
# WHY IT KNOWS IT
#
# HOW IT USES IT
#
# WHO CAN ACCESS IT
#
# HOW TO CORRECT IT
#
# HOW TO DELETE IT.
#
# ================================================================
# 665. "WHAT KAHIYE REMEMBERS"
# ================================================================
#
# Create a transparent memory interface.
#
# Categories:
#
# ABOUT YOUR CHILD
#
# ABOUT YOUR FAMILY
#
# CURRENT PRIORITIES
#
# ROUTINES
#
# DEVELOPMENTAL HISTORY
#
# STRATEGIES THAT HELP.
#
# ================================================================
# 666. NO HIDDEN PSYCHOLOGICAL PROFILE
# ================================================================
#
# Never maintain a secret:
#
# "Parent is neurotic"
#
# "Child is manipulative"
#
# "Marriage unstable"
#
# internal profile inaccessible to user.
#
# ================================================================
# 667. INFERRED MEMORY TRANSPARENCY
# ================================================================
#
# If Kahiye infers:
#
# "Transitions seem difficult lately,"
#
# parent should be able to:
#
# inspect
# correct
# reject
#
# that inference.
#
# ================================================================
# 668. PROFILE EXPLAINABILITY
# ================================================================
#
# For every meaningful inferred pattern answer:
#
# WHY DOES KAHIYE THINK THIS?
#
# Show:
#
# observations
# dates
# confidence
#
# without exposing unnecessary model internals.
#
# ================================================================
# 669. CHILD DEVELOPMENTAL PROFILE — DATA MODEL
# ================================================================
#
# Research a schema such as:
#
# identity
# developmental_window
# strengths
# interests
# current_skills
# emerging_skills
# routines
# preferences
# observed_tendencies
# challenges
# current_context
# significant_events
# interventions
# outcomes.
#
# ================================================================
# 670. NO FIXED PSYCHOLOGICAL ESSENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Developmental profile describes:
#
# CURRENTLY OBSERVED PATTERNS.
#
# It does NOT define:
#
# WHO THE CHILD IS FOREVER.
#
# ================================================================
# 671. PROFILE TEMPORALITY
# ================================================================
#
# Every behavioral descriptor should answer:
#
# WHEN?
#
# WHERE?
#
# HOW OFTEN?
#
# ================================================================
# 672. STRENGTH / CHALLENGE COEXISTENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Example:
#
# "Strong persistence"
#
# may coexist with:
#
# "difficulty shifting tasks."
#
# Avoid:
#
# positive/negative binary labeling.
#
# ================================================================
# 673. PROFILE CONFIDENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Each inferred tendency:
#
# LOW
# MEDIUM
# HIGH
#
# confidence.
#
# ================================================================
# 674. PROFILE EVIDENCE COUNT
# ================================================================
#
# Track:
#
# number of observations
# number of settings
# recency
# contradictory observations.
#
# ================================================================
# 675. CONTRADICTORY CHILD BEHAVIOR IS NORMAL
# ================================================================
#
# Child may be:
#
# confident at home
#
# and:
#
# quiet at school.
#
# Do not force:
#
# one global trait.
#
# ================================================================
# 676. CONTEXTUAL TRAITS
# ================================================================
#
# Store:
#
# behavior_by_context.
#
# ================================================================
# 677. DEVELOPMENTAL CHANGE DETECTION
# ================================================================
#
# Detect:
#
# emerging
# strengthening
# weakening
# resolved
#
# patterns.
#
# ================================================================
# 678. CHANGE ≠ PROGRESS IN ONE DIRECTION
# ================================================================
#
# Development can include:
#
# spurts
# plateaus
# temporary regressions
# context changes.
#
# ================================================================
# 679. LONGITUDINAL SUMMARY
# ================================================================
#
# Periodically summarize:
#
# WHAT HAS CHANGED?
#
# WHAT IS STABLE?
#
# WHAT HELPED?
#
# WHAT NEW SKILLS ARE EMERGING?
#
# WHAT MAY BE WORTH WATCHING?
#
# ================================================================
# 680. MONTHLY PARENTING REVIEW
# ================================================================
#
# Consider:
#
# "This month..."
#
# 3 meaningful developments
#
# 2 strategies that helped
#
# 1 emerging area
#
# 1 parent reflection.
#
# Avoid:
#
# grading.
#
# ================================================================
# 681. ANNUAL DEVELOPMENTAL STORY
# ================================================================
#
# Create a yearly:
#
# developmental narrative
#
# rather than:
#
# scorecard.
#
# Include:
#
# growth
# interests
# relationships
# challenges
# milestones
# memorable moments.
#
# ================================================================
# 682. CHILD MEMORY BOOK
# ================================================================
#
# Explore optional:
#
# private family memory book
#
# generated from:
#
# milestones
# photos
# parent notes
# funny moments
# achievements.
#
# Keep separate from:
#
# psychological assessment.
#
# ================================================================
# 683. MEMORY BOOK CONSENT
# ================================================================
#
# As child matures:
#
# give increasing control over:
#
# which memories/images are retained/shared.
#
# ================================================================
# 684. FAMILY JOURNAL
# ================================================================
#
# Parent may record:
#
# thoughts
# fears
# joys
# difficult days
# parenting reflections.
#
# This should not automatically become:
#
# child psychological profile.
#
# ================================================================
# 685. JOURNAL ANALYSIS
# ================================================================
#
# With permission, Kahiye may identify:
#
# parent stress patterns
# recurring triggers
# family themes.
#
# Keep:
#
# tentative
# transparent.
#
# ================================================================
# 686. JOURNAL SAFETY
# ================================================================
#
# Journal may reveal:
#
# depression
# violence
# self-harm
# abuse.
#
# Safety routing still applies.
#
# ================================================================
# 687. DAILY CHECK-IN
# ================================================================
#
# Optional parent check-in:
#
# ENERGY
#
# STRESS
#
# PATIENCE
#
# FAMILY LOAD.
#
# Use only if:
#
# it improves recommendations.
#
# ================================================================
# 688. PARENT STATE PERSONALIZATION
# ================================================================
#
# Same advice may be inappropriate when:
#
# parent is:
#
# exhausted
# ill
# overwhelmed.
#
# Kahiye may recommend:
#
# simplest safe intervention.
#
# ================================================================
# 689. NO MOOD DETERMINISM
# ================================================================
#
# Do not say:
#
# "You are stressed, therefore your interpretation is wrong."
#
# Parent state is:
#
# one contextual factor.
#
# ================================================================
# 690. PARENT WELLBEING TREND
# ================================================================
#
# With permission, detect:
#
# sustained stress
# exhaustion
# deteriorating mood
#
# and suggest:
#
# support
# rest
# professional help where appropriate.
#
# ================================================================
# 691. PARENT WELLBEING ≠ PRODUCTIVITY
# ================================================================
#
# Do not frame self-care solely as:
#
# "Recharge so you can parent better."
#
# Parent wellbeing has:
#
# intrinsic value.
#
# ================================================================
# 692. CO-PARENT LOAD
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# invisible labor
# mental load
# caregiving imbalance
# resentment.
#
# Help parents discuss:
#
# tasks
# ownership
# expectations.
#
# ================================================================
# 693. DOMESTIC LABOR
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid gender assumptions about:
#
# mother
# father
# household roles.
#
# ================================================================
# 694. FATHER ENGAGEMENT
# ================================================================
#
# Research father/caregiver engagement without treating fathers as:
#
# secondary helpers.
#
# ================================================================
# 695. NON-BIOLOGICAL CAREGIVERS
# ================================================================
#
# Recognize:
#
# adoptive parents
# step-parents
# guardians
# grandparents
# same-sex parents
# other caregivers.
#
# ================================================================
# 696. FAMILY TERMINOLOGY
# ================================================================
#
# Let family define:
#
# relationships
# names
# roles.
#
# ================================================================
# 697. MATERNAL DEFAULT FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# Do not assume:
#
# mother is primary caregiver
#
# unless context says so.
#
# ================================================================
# 698. PREGNANCY LANGUAGE
# ================================================================
#
# Use:
#
# medically clear
# respectful
# context-appropriate
#
# language.
#
# Allow user preference where relevant.
#
# ================================================================
# 699. POSTPARTUM FAMILY SYSTEM
# ================================================================
#
# Include:
#
# birthing parent
# partner
# infant
# siblings
# grandparents
# support network.
#
# ================================================================
# 700. NEW SIBLING TRANSITION
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# preparation
# jealousy
# regression
# connection
# role expectations.
#
# Avoid:
#
# "You're the big brother now, so you must..."
#
# as automatic emotional burden.
#
# ================================================================
# 701. MULTIPLE BIRTHS
# ================================================================
#
# Include:
#
# twins
# triplets
#
# and risks of:
#
# constant comparison
# identity fusion.
#
# ================================================================
# 702. TWIN INDIVIDUALITY
# ================================================================
#
# Treat each child as:
#
# individual.
#
# Avoid:
#
# "the outgoing twin"
# "the clever twin"
#
# permanent labels.
#
# ================================================================
# 703. PREMATURE BIRTH
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# corrected age
# developmental monitoring
# parent stress.
#
# Medical guidance must remain authoritative.
#
# ================================================================
# 704. BIRTH EXPERIENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Parent may have:
#
# traumatic
# complicated
# unexpected
#
# birth experience.
#
# Support:
#
# parent emotional processing
#
# without:
#
# diagnosing trauma.
#
# ================================================================
# 705. FEEDING EMOTION
# ================================================================
#
# Feeding choices can produce:
#
# guilt
# identity
# conflict.
#
# Kahiye should prioritize:
#
# child safety
# parent wellbeing
# evidence
#
# over:
#
# ideology.
#
# ================================================================
# 706. BREAST / FORMULA POLARIZATION FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# Do not shame:
#
# breastfeeding
# formula feeding
# mixed feeding.
#
# Use authoritative health guidance.
#
# ================================================================
# 707. PARENTING POLARIZATION
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should resist online:
#
# camps
# absolutism
# identity warfare.
#
# ================================================================
# 708. "BEST PARENTING METHOD" FIREWALL
# ================================================================
#
# There is rarely:
#
# one universal method
#
# for every:
#
# child
# age
# family
# context.
#
# ================================================================
# 709. DECISION SUPPORT
# ================================================================
#
# When evidence does not dictate one answer:
#
# provide:
#
# OPTIONS
#
# TRADE-OFFS
#
# QUESTIONS TO CONSIDER
#
# rather than:
#
# false certainty.
#
# ================================================================
# 710. PARENT DECISION MATRIX
# ================================================================
#
# For decisions such as:
#
# school
# phone
# extracurricular
# sleep arrangement
# allowance
# independence,
#
# consider:
#
# child needs
# safety
# family values
# evidence
# feasibility
# reversibility.
#
# ================================================================
# 711. REVERSIBILITY
# ================================================================
#
# Distinguish:
#
# easily reversible decisions
#
# from:
#
# high-consequence decisions.
#
# Encourage experimentation where:
#
# safe + reversible.
#
# ================================================================
# 712. NO DECISION OUTSOURCING
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye should not become:
#
# "AI decides how I raise my child."
#
# It should improve:
#
# parent judgment.
#
# ================================================================
# 713. EXPLAIN TRADE-OFFS
# ================================================================
#
# Example:
#
# giving teenager more phone privacy may support:
#
# autonomy/trust
#
# while reducing:
#
# parental visibility.
#
# Help parent reason.
#
# ================================================================
# 714. VALUE-SENSITIVE DECISIONS
# ================================================================
#
# Some questions cannot be resolved by:
#
# science alone.
#
# Example:
#
# religion
# family traditions
# allowance philosophy
# education philosophy.
#
# Separate:
#
# EVIDENCE
#
# from:
#
# FAMILY VALUES.
#
# ================================================================
# 715. ETHICAL RECOMMENDATION LAYER
# ================================================================
#
# For complex decisions:
#
# FACTS
#
# ↓
#
# DEVELOPMENTAL CONSIDERATIONS
#
# ↓
#
# SAFETY
#
# ↓
#
# FAMILY VALUES
#
# ↓
#
# OPTIONS
#
# ↓
#
# TRADE-OFFS.
#
# ================================================================
# 716. PARENT AGENCY
# ================================================================
#
# Final decision remains with:
#
# parent/guardian
#
# except where:
#
# immediate safety/legal obligations require escalation.
#
# ================================================================
# 717. CHILD AGENCY
# ================================================================
#
# As developmental capacity increases:
#
# child's voice should increasingly participate in:
#
# decisions affecting them.
#
# ================================================================
# 718. SHARED DECISION-MAKING
# ================================================================
#
# Research age-appropriate:
#
# shared decision-making
#
# especially around:
#
# activities
# school
# health
# devices
# independence.
#
# ================================================================
# 719. CHILD RIGHTS FRAMEWORK
# ================================================================
#
# Research relevant principles from:
#
# UN Convention on the Rights of the Child
#
# and applicable local law.
#
# Consider:
#
# dignity
# protection
# participation
# privacy
# development.
#
# ================================================================
# 720. RIGHTS ≠ ADULT RESPONSIBILITY
# ================================================================
#
# Child participation does not mean:
#
# child bears adult responsibility.
#
# ================================================================
# 721. SAFETY VS AUTONOMY
# ================================================================
#
# One of Kahiye's recurring reasoning tasks:
#
# HOW MUCH FREEDOM IS APPROPRIATE NOW?
#
# Model:
#
# risk
# capability
# maturity
# context
# past behavior
# support.
#
# ================================================================
# 722. AUTONOMY SHOULD GROW
# ================================================================
#
# Default long-term direction:
#
# increasing competence
#
# → increasing independence.
#
# ================================================================
# 723. OVERPROTECTION
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# anxiety
# parental accommodation
# risk aversion
# independence.
#
# Avoid blaming parents motivated by:
#
# legitimate safety concerns.
#
# ================================================================
# 724. FREE-RANGE CLAIMS
# ================================================================
#
# Evaluate:
#
# independence benefits
# environmental safety
# legal context
# culture.
#
# Do not adopt ideology.
#
# ================================================================
# 725. REAL-WORLD RISK
# ================================================================
#
# Guidance around:
#
# walking alone
# public transport
# staying home
# travel
#
# must consider:
#
# local environment
# child capability
# laws
# practical safety.
#
# # ================================================================
# 726. INDEPENDENCE READINESS ENGINE
# ================================================================
#
# Build a framework for parents deciding whether a child is ready for:
#
# walking to school alone
# staying home alone
# taking public transport
# managing keys
# attending activities without parent
# traveling short distances independently
# managing personal schedule
# handling money
# owning a smartphone
# independent online activity.
#
# Do NOT decide from:
#
# AGE ALONE.
#
# Evaluate:
#
# developmental capability
# judgment
# impulse control
# communication ability
# emergency knowledge
# environmental risk
# demonstrated responsibility
# local law
# family context.
#
# ================================================================
# 727. READINESS ≠ MATURITY LABEL
# ================================================================
#
# Avoid:
#
# "Your child is immature."
#
# Prefer:
#
# "Two skills still need practice before this particular responsibility
# becomes safer."
#
# ================================================================
# 728. READINESS SKILL GAP
# ================================================================
#
# If child is not ready:
#
# identify:
#
# WHICH CAPABILITY IS MISSING?
#
# Then create:
#
# practice plan.
#
# ================================================================
# 729. GRADUATED INDEPENDENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Instead of:
#
# NOT ALLOWED
#
# → FULL FREEDOM,
#
# design:
#
# SUPERVISED
# ↓
# PARTIALLY SUPERVISED
# ↓
# CHECK-IN BASED
# ↓
# INDEPENDENT.
#
# ================================================================
# 730. REAL-WORLD SAFETY CURRICULUM
# ================================================================
#
# Build age-progressive education for:
#
# roads
# strangers
# getting lost
# public transport
# emergency contacts
# unsafe situations
# basic first aid awareness
# asking trusted adults for help.
#
# Avoid:
#
# fear-based stranger-danger mythology.
#
# ================================================================
# 731. STRANGER DANGER AUDIT
# ================================================================
#
# Research modern child-safety guidance.
#
# Many risks involve:
#
# known people
#
# not:
#
# stereotypical strangers.
#
# Teach:
#
# unsafe behavior
# boundaries
# help-seeking
#
# rather than:
#
# simplistic stranger fear.
#
# ================================================================
# 732. SAFE ADULT NETWORK
# ================================================================
#
# Help families identify:
#
# multiple trusted adults
#
# child can approach.
#
# Do not make:
#
# one parent
#
# the child's only route to safety.
#
# ================================================================
# 733. FAMILY EMERGENCY PLAN
# ================================================================
#
# Build:
#
# emergency contacts
# meeting location
# medical information
# trusted adults
# communication plan.
#
# ================================================================
# 734. EMERGENCY SKILLS
# ================================================================
#
# Age-progressively teach:
#
# parent phone number
# home address where appropriate
# emergency numbers
# what to say in emergency
# basic safety actions.
#
# ================================================================
# 735. TRAVEL WITH CHILDREN
# ================================================================
#
# Research parenting guidance around:
#
# flights
# road trips
# unfamiliar places
# jet lag
# routines
# safety
# child identification.
#
# ================================================================
# 736. TEEN TRAVEL
# ================================================================
#
# For older adolescents:
#
# independent travel
# rides
# public transport
# parties
# emergency plans
# location sharing
# check-ins.
#
# ================================================================
# 737. PARTY SAFETY
# ================================================================
#
# Develop non-moralizing guidance around:
#
# transport
# substances
# consent
# peer pressure
# unsafe situations
# emergency pickup.
#
# ================================================================
# 738. "CALL ME, NO QUESTIONS UNTIL YOU'RE SAFE"
# ================================================================
#
# Research the value/limitations of family safety agreements where
# adolescent can seek:
#
# immediate rescue
#
# without fear of:
#
# immediate punishment.
#
# Safety first.
#
# Accountability can happen later.
#
# ================================================================
# 739. DRIVING / VEHICLE SAFETY
# ================================================================
#
# Where relevant by jurisdiction:
#
# learner driving
# helmets
# seatbelts
# distracted driving
# impaired driving
# riding with peers.
#
# ================================================================
# 740. SEXUAL SAFETY + PARTY CONTEXT
# ================================================================
#
# For older teens:
#
# consent
# intoxication
# coercion
# safe exit
# trusted contacts
#
# should be discussed:
#
# realistically
#
# rather than:
#
# through fear or shame.
#
# ================================================================
# 741. SUBSTANCE CONVERSATION COACH
# ================================================================
#
# Parent asks:
#
# "I found alcohol in my 16-year-old's bag."
#
# Kahiye should analyze:
#
# immediate safety
# context
# frequency
# source
# peer environment
# child's explanation
# family rules
# possible risk pattern.
#
# Do not jump directly to:
#
# addiction
#
# or:
#
# harmless experimentation.
#
# ================================================================
# 742. SUBSTANCE COMMUNICATION
# ================================================================
#
# Help parent combine:
#
# clear expectations
# factual risk
# listening
# safety planning
# proportional consequences.
#
# ================================================================
# 743. NO SCARE-TACTIC SCIENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Do not exaggerate:
#
# drug
# sex
# internet
# social media
#
# risks merely to frighten children into compliance.
#
# Loss of credibility can undermine future guidance.
#
# ================================================================
# 744. HEALTH LITERACY CURRICULUM
# ================================================================
#
# Teach age-progressively:
#
# body knowledge
# hygiene
# sleep
# movement
# nutrition
# medication safety
# healthcare communication
# mental-health literacy
# sexual/reproductive health.
#
# ================================================================
# 745. BODY AUTONOMY
# ================================================================
#
# Teach:
#
# child can have boundaries around:
#
# affection
# touch
# privacy
#
# while parents still retain responsibility for:
#
# necessary health
# hygiene
# safety.
#
# ================================================================
# 746. FORCED AFFECTION
# ================================================================
#
# Research guidance around:
#
# "Give uncle a hug."
#
# Help families preserve:
#
# cultural warmth
#
# without:
#
# overriding reasonable bodily boundaries.
#
# ================================================================
# 747. BODY SAFETY LANGUAGE
# ================================================================
#
# Research benefits of:
#
# accurate anatomical terminology
# age-appropriate body education.
#
# Avoid:
#
# shame
# secrecy
# euphemisms that impair communication.
#
# ================================================================
# 748. PRIVACY DEVELOPMENT
# ================================================================
#
# Teach:
#
# private spaces
# changing
# bathroom
# bodies
# digital privacy
#
# developmentally.
#
# ================================================================
# 749. CHILD MEDICAL CONSENT / ASSENT
# ================================================================
#
# Research age-appropriate:
#
# explanation
# assent
# participation
#
# while recognizing:
#
# parent/legal guardian authority
# jurisdictional requirements.
#
# ================================================================
# 750. HEALTHCARE SELF-ADVOCACY
# ================================================================
#
# Older adolescents should progressively learn to:
#
# describe symptoms
# ask questions
# understand medication
# schedule care where appropriate
# know relevant health history.
#
# ================================================================
# 751. TRANSITION TO ADULT HEALTHCARE
# ================================================================
#
# By late adolescence:
#

#
# increased responsibility for:

# consent
# privacy.

# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye's mission does not end by:
#
# abruptly stopping at birthday 18.
#
# Design a deliberate:
#
# TRANSITION-TO-ADULTHOOD EXPERIENCE.
#
# ================================================================
# 753. AGE-18 OBJECTIVES
# ================================================================
#
# By transition, ideally young person has increasing capability in:
#
# emotional self-management
# relationships
# health
# money
# digital safety
# learning
# decision-making
# practical independence
# help-seeking.
#
# Do not turn these into:
#
# graduation requirements.

# 754. KAHIYE HANDOVER
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# what information belongs to:
#
# PARENT
#
# versus:
#
# NOW-ADULT CHILD.
#
# ================================================================
# 755. CHILD ACCOUNT TRANSITION
# ================================================================
#
# Explore:
#
# whether adolescent can progressively receive:
#
# own Kahiye interface
#
# before:
#
# age 18.
#
# ================================================================
# 756. TEEN-FACING KAHIYE
# ================================================================
#
# If built:
#
# teen experience must NOT simply expose:
#
# parent's private notes
#
# or:
#
# hidden interpretations.
#
# Design:
#
# separate permissions
# separate memory
# transparent shared areas.
#
# ================================================================
# 757. PARENT-TEEN SHARED SPACE
# ================================================================
#
# Potential shared objects:
#
# family agreements
# goals
# routines
# upcoming events
# jointly agreed plans.
#
# ================================================================
# 758. PRIVATE PARENT SPACE
# ================================================================
#
# Parent may need:
#
# private reflection
#
# without:
#
# exposing every fear/frustration to child.
#
# ================================================================
# 759. PRIVATE TEEN SPACE
# ================================================================
#
# If legally/ethically appropriate:
#
# teen may need:
#
# private reflection
# questions
# education.
#
# Safety exceptions must be carefully researched and transparent.
#
# ================================================================
# 760. CONFIDENTIALITY EXPECTATIONS
# ================================================================
#
# Never promise:
#
# absolute confidentiality
#
# if system has:
#
# safety escalation
# legal obligations
# guardian access.
#
# Explain clearly.
#
# ================================================================
# 761. SHARED MEMORY PERMISSIONS
# ================================================================
#
# Every shared memory may have:
#
# PARENT ONLY
#
# CHILD/TEEN ONLY where appropriate
#
# SHARED
#
# PROFESSIONAL SHAREABLE.
#
# ================================================================
# 762. AGE-18 DATA CHOICE
# ================================================================
#
# Research UX allowing newly adult user to decide:
#
# keep
# transfer
# export
# delete
#
# appropriate parts of their developmental record.
#
# ================================================================
# 763. PARENT MEMORY AFTER 18
# ================================================================
#
# Parent may retain:
#
# their own journal
# family memories
#
# but should not automatically retain unrestricted control over:
#
# adult child's sensitive personal profile.
#
# ================================================================
# 764. PRODUCT NORTH STAR
# ================================================================
#
# Kahiye's north-star metric must NOT simply be:
#
# DAILY ACTIVE USERS
#
# TIME SPENT
#
# MESSAGES SENT.
#
# ================================================================
# 765. PARENTING OUTCOME METRICS
# ================================================================
#
# Research ethical measurable proxies such as:
#
# parent-reported usefulness
# increased confidence
# reduced uncertainty
# successful repair
# improved communication
# implementation of helpful routines
# reduced repetitive conflict
# appropriate professional help-seeking.
#
# ================================================================
# 766. NO CLAIMED CHILD OUTCOME WITHOUT EVIDENCE
# ================================================================
#
# Do not claim:
#
# "Kahiye makes children 40% more resilient"
#
# without rigorous evidence.
#
# ================================================================
# 767. PRODUCT IMPACT RESEARCH
# ================================================================
#
# Build a scientific evaluation program.
#
# Questions:
#
# Does Kahiye improve:
#
# parenting knowledge?
# parent self-efficacy?
# communication?
# stress?
# help-seeking?
#
# ================================================================
# 768. OUTCOME VALIDATION
# ================================================================
#
# Consider:
#
# pilot studies
# longitudinal studies
# expert review
# user-reported outcomes
# validated scales where legally/licensably appropriate.
#
# ================================================================
# 769. A/B TEST ETHICS
# ================================================================
#
# Do not A/B test:
#
# child safety
# crisis guidance
# evidence quality
#
# merely for:
#
# engagement.
#
# ================================================================
# 770. ENGAGEMENT VS WELLBEING
# ================================================================
#
# If:
#
# more notifications
#
# increase:
#
# app engagement
#
# but:
#
# increase parental anxiety,
#
# choose:
#
# wellbeing.
#
# ================================================================
# 771. BUSINESS MODEL ETHICS
# ================================================================
#
# Research sustainable monetization that does not require:
#
# exploiting parent fear

# addictive engagement
# hidden advertising.
#
# ================================================================
# 772. SUBSCRIPTION MODEL
# ================================================================
#
# Research:
#
# free
# freemium
# premium
# family plans
# professional plans
#
# based on:
#
# value
# accessibility
# sustainability.

# 773. FREE SAFETY LAYER
# ================================================================
#

#
# emergency/safeguarding information
#
# should not be hidden behind:

#

#
# affiliate
# sponsor
# commercial relationship.

# any advertising involving child profiles.

#

# 780. PARENT FEAR MARKETING FIREWALL

#
# In parenting:
